PRIOR (cycle 1768, opus-5) — 2026-10-15 ~21:55 UTC — **BUILD slot (1768 not divisible by 3).**
   `checks-due`: **NONE DUE** (21 tracked; soonest `check-git-push` in 19c, cycle 1787).
   `audit-due`: **NONE DUE** fleet-wide (soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` in 11c, cycle 1779).
   Dev.to NOT due (last publish 2026-10-14T07:33:08Z; next ~2026-10-16 07:33, ~10h out). Inbox:
   nothing owed (10 most recent all spam/auto-reply/DMARC/SEO-offer, same set as prior cycles).
   Cycle 1767 left **no hard carry-over**, so this cycle went to the one lever the strategy box
   names as actually mattering: **demand discovery for a real-demand niche that is HTTP-only
   feasible.** Result: 2 new niches found above/near the bars, both closed, and the whole band
   closed structurally.
   **(1) Scanned 27 unscanned keywords with `bin/store-scan` (free, ~2 min).** The public-JSON-API
   band is dead on demand (`goodreads` 4.3, `coingecko` 2.4, `tcg` 2.0, `npm` 1.8,
   `chrome extension` 1.6, `pokemon card` 1.6, `roblox` 1.2, `discogs` 1.0, `wordpress plugin` 0.9,
   `anime` 0.9, `myanimelist` 0.7, `itch.io` 0.6 — all vs the >= 50 bar). The non-US marketplace
   band is where demand is: **`shopee` d/c 58.6** (u30dSum 1583, topUsers 2948, ratio 0.54) and
   **`temu` d/c 48.7** (1364/1593, **ratio 0.86 — best beatable-leader figure of any
   feasible-looking niche; only infeasible `aliexpress` at 1.98 beats it**), then `ozon` 32.9,
   `mercadolibre` 30.4, `wildberries` 10.7, `shein` 9.8, `lazada` 9.1, rest <= 7.5. `shopee` is
   only the 2nd keyword this fleet has measured over the cycle-1488 d/c bar (after `etsy` 62.4) and
   the FIRST to clear bars 1 and 2 together (etsy's leader ratio was 0.04).
   **(2) Both died on bar 3 (HTTP-only feasibility), measured not assumed.** `temu`: direct from
   this box, EVERY path including `/robots.txt` returns a 2,897-byte obfuscated JS challenge — but
   **through `groups-BUYPROXIES94952` the same URLs return the real 286-304 KB page**, so Apify's
   IPs are not blocked; that body has no `__NEXT_DATA__`/`rawData`/ld+json blob at all (signed-XHR
   SPA, the AliExpress architecture). `shopee`: `/api/v4/search/search_items` → `403
   {"error":90309999}` on direct, `BUYPROXIES94952` AND `StaticUS3`, with and without browser
   `Referer`/`Accept`/`X-Requested-With`; search HTML is a 162 KB shell with no prices. Past
   90309999 means forging their anti-bot signature — rule 1, not attempted.
   **(3) The structural finding, which is the part worth keeping: `GET /v2/users/me` →
   `data.proxy.groups` shows this plan has `BUYPROXIES94952` (27 IPs) and `StaticUS3` (3), with
   `RESIDENTIAL`, `UNBLOCKER` and `GOOGLE_SERP` all at `availableCount: 0`.** Datacenter-only IPs
   + no headless browser (rule 7) is exactly the capability set the consumer retail/marketplace
   band is built to refuse — one explanation covering all 7 real-demand sites probed to date
   (cycle 1497's aliexpress/ebay/walmart/glassdoor/amazon + shopee/temu). **So the band is closed
   on a CAPABILITY, not site-by-site: re-open only if residential/Unblocker proxy becomes
   available (or gets a budget line), never on a new keyword alone.** Scan wide (free), probe
   narrow (costs a cycle).
   **(4) Shipped `bin/http-feasible <url...>`** so this probe is never hand-rolled again: fetches
   each URL on every route the plan actually has (direct + each group with `availableCount > 0`)
   and labels each body **DATA / SHELL / CHALLENGE / BLOCKED** with a per-URL verdict. Exists
   because the hand-rolled curl measured only this box's IP and would have filed temu's no-go under
   the wrong reason ("IP blocked" rather than "signed-XHR SPA"), which is the kind of error that
   makes a future cycle re-open on the wrong trigger. **Verified on 7 probes: reproduces both
   hand-measured verdicts exactly** (temu CHALLENGE direct / SHELL proxied / CHALLENGE StaticUS3;
   shopee BLOCKED 403 on all 3 routes) **plus a positive control** (`api.fda.gov` → DATA). Two of
   its own bugs were caught by those runs and fixed before commit: a single aggregate verdict for
   multi-URL batches, and a 2 KB byte-size floor that misread a populated 1,840-byte FDA response
   as SHELL (size is the right test for an embedded HTML blob, the wrong one for a JSON API — now
   tests for a non-empty row list at depth 1-2). Re-verified after both fixes incl. the zero-row
   case. No Actor code touched, **no builds, no Actor runs — spend this cycle is ~0** (a few dozen
   HTTP requests, ~1 MB of proxy traffic); `apify-spend` last projection stands at $7.34 vs the $85
   Bronze cap.
   3 services active (`fetchsmith-web`, `fetchsmith-mail`, `caddy`); site 200 on `/`, `/pricing`,
   `/tools`. No owner email (no revenue event, no outage). Revenue unchanged: **$0 booked, 24
   Actors, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews, 45 (windowed/platform-artifact) users, 662 runs30d / 659 ext_ok**
   — and per `bin/revenue`'s own caveat those runs are non-billable platform traffic, not demand.

   every `pushed === 0`/`!pushed` branch and checks whether ANY of them (not every one — a
   per-ACTOR verdict, to avoid over-reporting nested diagnostic sub-branches or `Actor.fail()`
   exits as misses) reaches `Actor.setStatusMessage`, directly or via a message-like variable
   referenced in a later call. First raw pass flagged 24 branches across 11 Actors; manually
   confirmed most were false positives (nested detail logs already covered by a broader sibling
   branch, or `Actor.fail()` paths — a different, already-correct mechanism) on

   `sec-insider-trades-scraper`, then re-scoped to per-actor and tuned out the `Actor.fail()`
   case, landing on **4 real MISS**: `clinicaltrials-scraper`, `fda-recall-scraper`,
   `grants-gov-scraper`, `nih-reporter-scraper` — none of these were in the 1752-1757 audit's
   hand-picked list, despite being equally search-shaped (keyword/date/agency filters, watch
   mode). Registered in `state/fleet_checks.json`'s `not_cadence.on_demand_per_actor` (same

Shipped a real buyer-facing fix to `trademark-search-scraper`: a zero-row path that produced NO status message at all.** All rotations re-verified NONE DUE (`checks-due` soonest `check-row-floor` in 12c; `audit-due` competitor_audit NONE DUE soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` in 27c). 3 services active, site 200 on `/`, `/pricing`, `/tools`. Inbox nothing owed (10 most recent all spam/auto-replies/DMARC/SEO offers); `bin/devto-comments` re-run per QUALITY-slot convention — only the standing won't-reply set (1 bot, 2 content-free praise), no new comment. No owner email (no revenue event, no outage). Revenue unchanged: **$0 booked, 24 Actors, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews, 44 users.** dev.to post NOT yet due (last 2026-10-14T07:33Z, next ~2026-10-16 07:33Z, ~17.5h out). **Spend: 3 Apify builds (~$0.015) + 8 cheap runs; `bin/apify-spend` projects $7.13 this billing cycle (2026-10-09..11-08) against the $120 line — $1.51 used, $0.51 above included credits.** Three pushes, two more than the standard one/Actor/cycle: 0.1.58 shipped the fix, 0.1.59 a plural/singular grammar fix in copy a buyer reads, 0.1.60 a README clause added after 0.1.59 that the Store page would otherwise have omitted. Verified via the `latest` build's `readme` field (not the CDN-cached page) that 0.1.60 carries both README edits.

**Found by following a live run burst, not the rotation.** `bin/usage-trend` showed `trademark-search-scraper` at **+4 runs in 25h** vs a +0/+1 fleet baseline; its per-snapshot series pinned `runs30d` 24 -> 27 between 10-14 13:00Z and 10-14 15:35Z — **+3 successful external runs in ~2.5h** on an Actor trending ~+1/day. That is the one signal `bin/revenue`'s caveat calls usable (per-Actor deviation from the non-billable baseline, same shape as court-records' 10-02 burst behind the 1731 copy fix). It read as one person iterating on inputs, so the cycle went to that Actor's buyer experience rather than to `steam-reviews-scraper` (what the `varied_test` rotation would have picked, 44c out).

**The defect, live-reproduced BEFORE any code change:** `pushed === 0` with every filter value VALID finished SUCCEEDED with **`statusMessage: null`**. The Actor had two well-written zero-row branches (invalid filter values; `stoppedByCap`) and a genuinely empty search fell through both — Console showed a green run beside an empty dataset with zero explanation, and since nothing is charged there is no bill to ask about either. Repro: `searchTerm "qwertzuiopx"` + `offices JP` + class 45 + status `Expired` -> `declaredMatches 0 / delivered 0 / complete true`, all three `unknown*` arrays empty, no status message.

**Fix shipped (builds 0.1.58 + 0.1.59) and verified live across 7 runs / all 4 branches, 2 of them regressions:** (a) valid-filters-empty -> new message naming the filters actually applied and telling the buyer to drop/widen one at a time; (b) no-filters-empty -> new message; (c) `offices:["UK"]` -> the ORIGINAL invalid-value message, unchanged; (d) `solar`/`US`/`maxResults 3` -> 3 rows charged and the ORIGINAL capped message, unchanged. New branch is gated `!watchMode` (watch mode's own "nothing new since last run" message is better and is set earlier — the new branch would have overwritten it) and `runError === null` (a transport failure reports via `Actor.fail()`; calling it "genuinely empty" would be the 1637 wrong-cause bug). Advice is split by which knob can actually fix it: filters set -> name them; none set -> say so, because then the TERM is what must change. README gained an FAQ covering all three zero-row causes.

**Unplanned finding worth keeping: Apify applies `input_schema` DEFAULTS server-side.** A run posted with no `offices` key came back saying "...once offices US, EM is applied" — the schema's `offices` default. Message is still correct (clearing `offices` widens to all 81), but never infer which branch a run takes from the JSON you posted; read the schema defaults too. README FAQ now states that default by name. Also re-confirmed the 1636 pre-finish snapshot trap a third time: the capped run read `{result: 0}` on first GET, `{result: 3}` on re-GET.

"AI Agent" in a remote-jobs-scraper title fails that twice: a human reader would infer "scrapes AI agent jobs" or "is an AI agent" — neither true — and even the charitable true reading ("callable by an AI agent via HTTP," true of every Actor in the fleet) doesn't convert, since everyone else ranking for that query IS an agent/MCP product and that buyer wants to deploy an agent, not scrape job boards. Full writeup: LEARNINGS cycle 1749, NICHES.md sub-finding under "MCP-server packaging — EVALUATED AND CLOSED." **No edit shipped, no build.** This closes the queue-1748 item; re-open only if a specific Actor's real output would make an agent-client phrase narrowly and specifically true, not as a generic rank play.

Revenue unchanged ($0 booked, 24 Actors, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews, 44 users; 652 runs30d / 649 ext_ok30d = the known non-billable platform baseline). Inbox: nothing owed (same spam/auto-replies/DMARC/SEO "list your domain" offers). No owner email sent (no revenue event, no outage). **Spend: $0 — read-only Apify GETs and `/v2/store` searches only, no Actor runs, no `apify push`, no builds.**

**Spent the BUILD slot measuring the one deferred idea that attacks the actual $0-revenue constraint (unit economics + the agent-buyer channel) rather than re-deriving the closed niche hunt for the 7th time: MCP-server packaging. Verdict NO-GO, with numbers, so it is never re-derived.** Four prior `competitor_audit` cycles (526, 1021, 1227/1260, plus two archive notes) each wrote it off verbatim as "a different product shape, not an input-field gap, out of scope this cycle" and nobody ever measured it. Full record in LEARNINGS cycle 1748 + NICHES.md "MCP-server packaging — EVALUATED AND CLOSED".

**The price lever is real and the largest ever measured in this fleet:** live `pricingInfos` (filtered `startedAt <= now`) show flat per-tool-call prices of **$0.01** (`makework36/airbnb-mcp-server`), **$0.02** (`makework36/reddit-mcp-server` — a *single* `mcp-tool-call` event, no per-row event at all) and **$0.03** (`kazkn/vinted-mcp-server`, plus $0.0001 each for `list`/`resource`/`prompt`/`completion` requests, i.e. MCP protocol chatter is itself billable). The whole fleet bills **$0.0001-$0.004 per ROW**. `mrbridge/espn-mcp-server` and `mrbridge/latest-news-mcp-server` return `eventPriceUsd: None` on every event — the tiered shape, cycle-480 trap — so **their prices are not established and were not quoted.**

**But demand follows the DATA, not the packaging — which is why it's a NO-GO.** Every MCP listing with real traction is consumer-facing or news (`espn` 249 users/73 u30d, `vinted` 112/17, `latest-news` 111/32, `opentable-booker` 103/20, `airbnb` 96/35, `reddit` 78/28, `skyscanner` 75/18 — individually 2-6x our ENTIRE fleet's 44 users), and they sit on exactly the bot-hardened consumer sites cycle 1489 already screened out as not HTTP-only feasible. In our own categories the MCP versions are as dead as the per-row ones (`store-scan` demand/competition: `legal mcp` 1.6, `finance mcp` 1.6, `patent mcp` 1.0, `government contracts mcp` 0.9, `weather mcp` 0.7, `clinical trials mcp` 0.5, `grants mcp` 0.5, `sec mcp` 0.4; top listing in each 3-34 users). The price lever multiplies a buyer count that is ~0 in every niche we can legally and HTTP-only reach.

**The decisive test, and the generalisable lesson: a same-data control.** The tempting read was that `martc03/court-records-mcp` (34 users, the `legal mcp` leader) proves MCP packaging would fix `court-records-scraper` — our largest standing real-demand gap (+33.6 excess runs30d, 1 user, flagged since 1731). **It proves the opposite: that listing is `FREE` (earns nothing), sits at u30d=1 and last ran 2026-10-08, while two PER-ROW rivals beat it outright in the same niche — `automation-lab/court-records-scraper` 77 users/13 u30d and `nexgendata/court-records-search` 63/3.** Holding the data constant, the per-row shape wins and the MCP shape is stagnant and unmonetised. **Rule recorded: when a rival's different product SHAPE looks like the explanation for their user count, find a listing of each shape on the SAME data before believing it — otherwise you are comparing niches and calling it packaging.**

**One cheap sub-finding left genuinely open (queued for 1749, expected to fail):** the traction leaders all name AI clients in the TITLE ("Reddit MCP Server — Claude, ChatGPT, Cursor, Codex"); ours name only the data source. `store-scan` on client names gives demand/competition `chatgpt` 21.9, `claude` 14.1, `ai agent` 9.6, `cursor` 7.5 (only 12 listings, u30dSum 97, top hit an MCP listing) against 0.4-1.6 on every one of our niche terms. But these are head queries with giant unrelated incumbents (`claude-code` 7135 users) and cycles 1040/1049 found the copy lever exhausted on head queries — so it is queued as a free, reversible, *measured* test (one Actor's title/description, `check-store-index` 0-stale first per cycle 240/972, then a `bin/store-rank` tracked-query delta), not as a plan. **Also flagged unmeasured rather than assumed:** whether these listings run in Apify standby mode — the fields probed (`isStandby`/`standbyConfig`/`defaultRunOptions.standby`) are absent from the public Actor record, so that read was empty, not negative.

**No new Actor built** (BUILD slot): niche hunting stays closed (1703/1720, re-verified 1742/1747), and this cycle's measurement is the better use of the slot — it permanently closes a repeatedly-deferred strategic question with live numbers instead of producing a 25th Actor into a capped niche.

**Fixed a buyer-facing price that no buyer could pay, on all 24 tool pages.** `/tools/<slug>` quoted its price as "1 credit per result / About $0.0025 per result on the Starter pack" — the **hosted-API credit price, for a Polar checkout that is not live** (`checkout_live = bool(polar_products())`, deferred by owner decision). The only destination a buyer can actually buy from is the Apify Actor the page's own CTA points at, and **its live price was nowhere on the page or in the page's schema.org `Offer`**. Because the credit price is a flat $0.0025/result for every tool (1 credit x $0.0025) while real live Apify prices span **$0.0001-$0.004**, the page **overstated the price 25x** on `app-store-reviews-scraper` and `google-play-reviews-scraper` and understated it on `us-federal-awards-scraper`.

This is the unfinished half of the standing cycle-1653 growth conclusion, which has read as "closed" since: our off-platform channel is AI answer engines (out-crawling search **14.6:1** as of 1676), and the question to optimise is *"when an answer engine summarises this page, does the buyer get a clickable, billable destination AND the price?"*. 1653 fixed the destination (Actor URL as its own anchor text); the price half was never picked up. An answer engine citing a tool page was pairing a correct URL with a fabricated price.

**Shipped:** `bin/sync-apify-prices` (pulls each public Actor's live effective primary-event price into `registry.json` as `apify_price {event, shape, entry, floor}`; `--check` is a read-only drift tripwire, registered in `state/fleet_checks.json` at 336c/7d, plus a PLAYBOOK step-7 event-trigger to re-run after any price change). `meta.json` **cannot** be the source — 14 of 30 charge events carry no `eventPriceUsd` there (flat-vs-tiered shape, the cycle-480 trap), so the live `pricingInfos` record is authoritative, filtered `startedAt <= now` (cycle-388 trap). `templates/tool.html`: while checkout is not live, the Apify price is the headline and the credit price is demoted and explicitly marked not-open-yet; the `Offer` now carries the Apify price, the Actor URL and `InStock`. Quotes the **entry** price with the floor as a secondary clause ("dropping to $X on higher plans") — the phrasing the READMEs already use; quoting the floor alone would advertise ~2x cheaper than a new buyer is charged. `app.py` `_usd` filter: `'%.4f'` would have rendered 0.000575 as "0.0006" and 0.00085 as "0.0009", i.e. invented prices.

**Verified:** 24/24 priced from the live record, re-check 0 drift (idempotent); registry diff **additions only** (checked that `indent=2, ensure_ascii=False` round-trips byte-identically before writing, so the change is not buried in 420 bytes of em-dash escaping); rendered text **and** JSON-LD checked against live platform prices on a flat Actor (trademark $0.002) and a tiered one (steam $0.000575/$0.0003); the `checkout_live=True` and `retired` branches rendered offline and both correct; **"a run that returns nothing costs nothing" verified true before asserting it** — 0 of 24 Actors has a one-time start-fee charge event. `check-registry-fields`, `check-pricing` (24 Actors/29 events), `check-links` (114 pages), `check-meta-fields`, `check-cta-presence` all clean; 23 tool pages resubmitted to IndexNow (200). PRIOR (cycle 1741, sonnet-5) — 2026-10-15 ~08:10 UTC — **BUILD-slot cycle (1741 not divisible by 3). All rotations re-verified NONE DUE** (`checks-due` soonest `check-git-push` 1746, 5c; `audit-due` competitor_audit NONE DUE soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 38c; `--type varied_test` NONE DUE). 3 services active, site 200 on `/`. Revenue/inbox unchanged ($0 booked, 24 Actors, inbox still only spam/auto-replies/DMARC, no reply owed). No owner email sent (no revenue event, no outage). **Spend this cycle: 1 build + 1 tiny platform run + several local runs.**

**Fixed the real bug cycle 1740 flagged: `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`'s enrich-before-push ordering (platform build 0.1.53).** The old code ran two full passes over the entire `results` array — `enrichOne()` then `fetchAttachments()`, one HTTP call + 200ms sleep per row each — BEFORE the push/charge loop ever started. A platform hard kill during either pass lost 100% of the run's rows and wrote no RUN_SUMMARY, on an ordinary large enriched run with no upstream slowness at all. **Fix: moved both enrichment calls inside the per-item push loop, immediately before each row is decided on (watch-skip or `pushResult`)**, so enrichment and pushing now happen one row at a time — a hard kill loses only the not-yet-reached tail, same as a run with enrichment off, matching the model the rest of the fleet (`pushResult()`-per-row) already uses. The `enrich-failed`/`attachments-failed` `markIncomplete()` checks moved to after the loop (they can't be evaluated until the loop runs now) but kept their exact original first-cause-wins priority order (max-results-from-reach, then enrich-failed, then attachments-failed, then charge-limit/max-results-from-loop) — and their denominator changed from `results.length` to `index` (rows actually attempted before an early break), which is the more accurate count when the loop breaks early on `maxResults`/charge-limit.

**Verified:** `node --check` clean; a normal local run (`apify run --input-file=test_input.json`) matched a `git stash` pre-fix run's RUN_SUMMARY exactly (same 15/15 rows, same `max-results` completeness message) — the per-row dataset CONTENT differed between the two runs, but re-running the unmodified post-fix code twice in a row produced the identical kind of row-level difference, proving it's SAM.gov's own live relevance-sorted paging (same pre-existing nondeterminism cycle 1738 already documented), not a regression from this fix. **The actual fix verified directly: a hard `kill -9` ~3s into a local run (mid-`enrichOne` pass) left 2 dataset rows already written to disk** — under the pre-fix code this same kill would leave 0 (enrichment for all 15 rows had to finish before the push loop, which writes `Actor.pushData`, ever ran once). Then `apify push --force` (build **0.1.53**) + a real `apify call` against the unmodified `test_input.json` matched pre-fix output exactly (15 rows, same `max-results` message, no live regression). `check-charges` 24/24 clean, `check-meta-fields` 0 stale (no meta/schema changes, code-only fix).

**This closes the rollout's last open item — all reliability gaps the timeout-guard rollout (cycles 1733-1740) and this follow-up found are now fixed across the fleet.** No new Actor built this cycle (code-only fix on an existing Actor); next BUILD-slot cycle should either build a new Actor (check `apify-admin store` first per PLAYBOOK pace rule) or pick up general QUALITY/GROWTH work — see queue.md for the open items list (none urgent).

PRIOR (cycle 1740, opus-5) — 2026-10-15 ~07:55 UTC — **QUALITY/GROWTH-slot cycle (1740 = 3×580), spent on the last reliability fix of the timeout-guard rollout. All rotations re-verified NONE DUE** (`checks-due` soonest `check-git-push` 1746, 6c; `audit-due` competitor_audit NONE DUE soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 39c; `--type varied_test` NONE DUE soonest `steam-reviews-scraper` 1792, 52c). 3 services active, site 200 on `/`, `/pricing`, `/tools`. Revenue/inbox unchanged ($0 booked, 24 Actors, inbox still only spam/auto-replies/DMARC, no reply owed). No owner email sent (no revenue event, no outage). **Spend this cycle: 1 build + 1 tiny platform run + 6 local runs.**

**THE TIMEOUT-GUARD ROLLOUT IS COMPLETE — `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` was the 8th and last Actor (platform build 0.1.74).** Added the standard `timeoutAt`/`remainingMs()`/`timeBudgetOk()` infra, an up-front budget check at the top of each `fetchPage` attempt (sets `source.lastError = 'run-timeout budget exceeded'`, returns `null` rather than firing a doomed request), and capped both retry-sleep branches. The 429 branch was the worst `Retry-After` exposure found anywhere in the fleet: it honoured an uncapped server header **and defaulted to a full 120s** when the header was absent, across 4 attempts.

**A new sleep shape the other 7 Actors did not have: a rate-limit SELF-throttle (`RATE_WINDOW_MS`, up to ~60.5s) that must NOT be capped.** Shortening that sleep to fit the budget would wake early and fire a request breaching the portal's own published rate limit — trading a clean stop for a 429 (or a reputational block). So this one site stops the source instead: `if (waitMs > remainingMs())` → set `timeBudgetExceeded`, return `null`. **Generalises: cap retry/backoff sleeps, but a sleep that exists to satisfy someone else's rate limit is a correctness constraint, not a delay — convert it to an early exit, never a shorter wait.**

**Also fixed an adjacent misleading-advice bug the forced-timeout test exposed** (same class as cycle 1736's clinicaltrials finding). On a 0-row run this Actor logged a 5-cause filter checklist ("cpvCodes does not match", "updatedWithinDays is too short", ...) — every one of which the clock-cut run never actually tested — and in watch mode logged "Nothing new... that is the expected result most of the time; you were charged for nothing", which reassures a buyer their watch is healthy when it never finished looking. Both now sit behind a `timeBudgetExceeded` branch that says so plainly and tells the buyer NOT to change filters on the strength of that run. The `seedFailure` gate also grew a `SEED_FATAL_REASONS` set so `'time-budget'` joins `'source-failed'` in refusing to save a baseline (the page/seed caps stay excluded — the buyer picks those deliberately, whereas the clock cuts at an arbitrary, run-to-run-varying point).

**Verified 6 ways:** (1) normal local run **byte-identical** to a pre-fix baseline run on both the dataset (15/15 rows) and RUN_SUMMARY; (2) forced-timeout normal mode → clean rc=0, `incompleteReason: "time-budget"`; (3) forced-timeout watch/seed → `Actor.fail` rc=1 and the `fetchsmith-uk-tender-watch` KV store directory was never even created (confirmed with `find`, 0 `watch-*` keys, not just the log's claim); (4) **mid-walk timeout on a wide query — the most informative test: Find a Tender's page 1 completed and all 75 already-fetched rows were still delivered and charged, then the clock stopped the walk and the reason correctly named Contracts Finder as the source still paging.** Already-paid-for work is preserved, not discarded; (5) a 120s-future budget ran identically to baseline — no false trigger at a realistic margin; (6) `apify push --force` (build **0.1.74**) + a real `apify call` on the unmodified `test_input.json` matched pre-fix output exactly (15 rows, 2 pages, 55 scanned, `max-results`). `check-meta-fields` 0 stale, `check-store-meta` 0 drift, `check-charges` 0 missing, `check-seed-save` 0 suspect, `check-fail-ordering` 0 suspect.

**Fleet-wide completion claim, verified rather than assumed** (grepped all 25 dirs for the infra + uncapped `await sleep(`): the unbounded-sleep/hard-kill class is now closed everywhere. The 4 Actors without the infra are the ones triaged as never exposed — `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` (caps at 30s), `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` (no manual sleep loop; got's bounded `maxRetryAfter`), `scholarship-scraper` (no sleeps), `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` (all sleeps hardcoded 200ms–3s, server `Retry-After` ignored entirely, ~20s worst case per call site).

**But that sam-gov triage, re-checked this cycle, was incomplete — it looked only at sleep DURATIONS and missed a worse hard-kill shape. New open task, see queue.md.** `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` runs its `enrichDetail`/`includeAttachments` loops (1 HTTP call + a 200ms sleep **per row**, no time budget anywhere) over the entire result set at lines ~1248–1265, and only reaches its `Actor.pushData`/`Actor.charge` push loop at line ~1276 — i.e. **every row is enriched before any row is pushed**. A platform hard kill mid-enrichment therefore loses 100% of the run's rows and writes no RUN_SUMMARY: strictly worse than the unbounded-sleep bug this rollout just closed, and reachable on an ordinary large enriched run without any upstream slowness at all. Not an unbounded hang, so the old triage was technically right and practically wrong.

**Simplest shape of the rollout so far.** This Actor already passes a per-call-site `state` object into `apiGet` (`state.lastError`/`state.lastErrorStatus`), and its partial-baseline guard (`if (watchMode && seeding && runState.failed) { ...don't save... }`) keys off `runState.failed` directly rather than off a specific `incompleteReason` string — so the existing code ALREADY refuses to save a baseline on a clock-caused stop, with zero changes needed to that gate. **One side effect worth recording for the next Actor's triage:** `resolveAgencies()` calls `apiGet` without passing a `state` object (by design — it has its own graceful fallback), so when the budget is already exhausted before it runs, it logs "Could not load the agency list; passing agency values through unvalidated" rather than a time-budget message. Confirmed this is harmless (same fallback that already existed for a slow/dead agency-list endpoint) and does not affect `runState`/`incompleteReason`.

**Verified thoroughly:** (1) normal local run matched a `git stash` pre-fix run byte-for-byte (`diff` on both full JSON and `documentNumber` order, 12/12 rows, zero diff); (2) forced-timeout normal mode (`ACTOR_TIMEOUT_AT` 20s future) → clean rc=0, status message `time-budget`, RUN_SUMMARY confirms `incompleteReason: "time-budget"`; (3) forced-timeout watch/seed mode → `Actor.fail` rc=1 with correct "re-run in a few minutes" advice, and the `fetchsmith-fedreg-watch` KV store directory was never even created — confirmed via `ls`, not just the log; (4) a 120s-future budget (past the 45s margin) ran the real `test_input.json` normally, matching the original baseline exactly (12/12 rows) — no false trigger at a realistic margin. Then `apify push --force` (build **0.1.50**) + a real `apify call` on the unmodified `test_input.json` matched pre-fix output exactly (12 rows of 12 scanned). `check-meta-fields` 0 stale, `check-store-meta` 0 drift, `check-charges` 0 missing.

Revenue/inbox unchanged ($0 booked, 24 Actors, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews; inbox still only spam/auto-replies/DMARC, no reply owed). The pre-flight-checked 2026-10-16 ~07:33 UTC dev.to post is still ~1 day out, not due; `bin/traffic` out_clicks still deliberately unread (not due before ~2026-10-18). **Continued the timeout-guard rollout onto `us-federal-awards-scraper` (5th Actor fixed, platform build 0.1.69).** Added the standard `timeoutAt`/`remainingMs()`/`timeBudgetOk()` infra, capped both `postPage` retry-sleep branches (the 429/5xx one honoured an uncapped server `Retry-After`) at `remainingMs()`, and short-circuited the outer per-category `for` loop once `timeBudgetExceeded`.

**Found and fixed a real bug the forced-timeout seed test exposed, not present in the first 4 Actors fixed this rollout:** this Actor's `assertFiltersApplied()` pre-walk filter-canary probes call the SAME `postPage()` as the main walk, and run BEFORE it. With a tight budget, the probes alone can exhaust `remainingMs()` and set `timeBudgetExceeded = true` before the main category walk ever starts — so the walk's own `seedError` flag (set only inside its while-loop body) never gets set, and `seedFailure = seeding && seedError` read false even though nothing was actually walked. First test run (before this fix) logged "Baseline saved... 0 charged" and **actually wrote a `watch-*` KV record** for a run that never walked a single page — exactly the false-complete-baseline bug this whole rollout exists to prevent. Fixed by changing the gate to `seedFailure = seeding && (seedError || timeBudgetExceeded)`. **Generalises: when a shared retry-wrapped fetch function is called from more than one call site (a pre-flight probe AND the main walk), a time-budget flag set by either site must feed the SAME completeness gate — don't assume the main walk is the only place the clock can run out.**

**Verified 4 ways + platform:** (1) normal local run **byte-identical** dataset vs. a real `git stash` pre-fix run (`CRAWLEE_STORAGE_DIR`, not `APIFY_LOCAL_STORAGE_DIR`, which this box's apify/crawlee versions silently ignore — re-confirmed the PLAYBOOK warning is still accurate); (2) forced-timeout normal mode (20s budget) -> clean rc=0, 0 rows, no hang; (3) forced-timeout watch/seed mode -> **first exposed the bug above** (bad: baseline falsely saved), then after the fix, confirmed rc=1, `Actor.fail` with "ran out of run-timeout budget... raise the run timeout or narrow the filters", and **no `watch-*` KV key written**; (4) a 50s-budget run with the real small `test_input.json` completed normally (rc=0, same 12/22 output) — confirms the guard doesn't false-trigger at a realistic margin. Then `apify push --force` (build **0.1.69**) + a real `apify call` on the unmodified `test_input.json` matched pre-fix output exactly (12 rows of 22 scanned). `check-meta-fields` 0 stale, `check-store-meta` 0 drift, `check-charges` 0 missing.

**Rollout list: 3 Actors remain** (`grants-gov-scraper`, `federal-register-scraper`, `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`), plus `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` at medium priority (bounded waits already; gains only the proactive stop, not an unbounded-hang fix). **New triage step for all 3, learned this cycle: check whether the target Actor has a pre-walk filter-verification/probe step that shares the same retry-wrapped fetch function as the main walk** — if so, the `seedFailure`/completeness gate must OR in the `timeBudgetExceeded` flag directly, not just the walk's own locally-set error flag, or re-run the forced-timeout seed test until it actually fails closed (don't trust a clean "Baseline saved" log without checking the KV store for the written key, as this cycle did).

PRIOR (cycle 1736, opus-5) — 2026-10-15 ~05:40 UTC — **BUILD-slot cycle (1736 not divisible by 3). `apify-spend` was DUE and ran: USED $1.4305 of the $85 hard cap, 20.1% of the billing cycle elapsed, cycle projection $7.12 — no concern** (recorded via `checks-due --ran`, next due 1832). `audit-due` competitor_audit NONE DUE (soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 43c); `--type varied_test` NONE DUE (soonest `steam-reviews-scraper` 1792, 56c). 3 services active (`fetchsmith-web`/`fetchsmith-mail`/`caddy`), site 200 on `/`, `/pricing`, `/tools`, `/tools/clinicaltrials-scraper`. **Continued the timeout-guard rollout onto `clinicaltrials-scraper` — 4th Actor fixed, platform build 0.1.69.** Added the `timeoutAt`/`remainingMs()`/`timeBudgetOk()`/`failureReason()` infra, capped both `apiGet` retry-sleep branches (the 429/5xx one honoured an uncapped server `Retry-After`) at `remainingMs()`, and routed the 3 upstream-failure `markIncomplete()` sites (`search-request-failed` ×2, `lookup-request-failed`) through `failureReason()` so a clock stop reports `time-budget`. The shape-specific hazard cycle 1735 flagged was real: the budget branch must set `lastApiErrorStatus = null`, since `markIncomplete()` defaults its `status` from that module-level var and `seedFailureIsInputError` tests it against `INPUT_ERROR_STATUS` — a stale 4xx would have told the buyer to fix filter values that were never wrong. Also added `'time-budget'` to `SEED_UPSTREAM_FAILURES`, short-circuited `resolveIdsChunk`'s binary split once the budget is gone, and gave both buyer-facing seed-failure messages a third advice branch ("raise the run timeout or narrow the query") instead of "wait for ClinicalTrials.gov to answer again".

**Verified 5 local scenarios + the platform:** byte-identical dataset AND RUN_SUMMARY vs. a real `git stash` pre-fix run; forced-timeout normal mode (clean rc=0, `time-budget`, `incompleteStatus: null`); forced-timeout mid-walk (2 full pages kept, run SUCCEEDS, `2,000 row(s) of 123,984 declared — time-budget`); forced-timeout watch/seed (no baseline saved, no `watch-*` key, `Actor.fail` rc=1 with the new advice); forced-timeout lookup mode (all 4 nctIds -> `failedIds`, not notFound/malformed, split short-circuit confirmed by only 2 WARN lines). Then `apify push --force` + a real `apify call` on the unmodified `test_input.json` matched pre-fix output exactly (12 rows of 206, `max-results`), proving the guard does not fire spuriously where `timeoutAt` IS set. `check-meta-fields` 0 stale, `check-store-meta` 0 drift, `check-charges` 0 missing, `check-seed-save` 0 suspect.

**BROKEN TEST RECIPE CORRECTED (the last 3 cycles recorded a form that silently no-ops on this box):** `date -u -d '+20s' --iso-8601=seconds` errors with `invalid date` here, so `ACTOR_TIMEOUT_AT` got the EMPTY STRING and the first forced-timeout test passed as an ordinary run. Use `date -u -d '20 seconds' --iso-8601=seconds` (no `+`), keep the offset under the 45s margin to fire immediately (or ~46-55s to stop mid-walk), and **always confirm the run actually reported `time-budget` rather than trusting a clean exit.**

**Rollout list is down to 4 genuinely-exposed Actors, not 7 — 2 were struck this cycle by reading their retry code:** `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` STRUCK (no manual sleep loop; got's own bounded `retry`/`maxRetryAfter`) and `eu-ted-tenders-scraper` STRUCK to low priority (already caps its own `Retry-After` at 30s). Still exposed: `us-federal-awards-scraper`, `grants-gov-scraper`, `federal-register-scraper`, `uk-find-a-tender-scraper`; `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` is medium (bounded waits, gains only the proactive stop). See `queue.md` for the 30-second triage grep and the four reference implementations.

Revenue/inbox unchanged ($0 booked, 24 Actors, 44 users, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews, same spam/auto-reply/DMARC messages, no reply owed). No owner email (no revenue event, no outage). **Spend: 1 build + 1 tiny platform run**; `bin/apify-spend` shows $1.4167 used of the $85 hard cap (19.9% of billing cycle elapsed) — no concern.

**Shipped item (a) from cycle 1732's follow-up list: gave `court-records-scraper` the soft-deadline timeout guard that 12 of 24 Actors already carry, copied from `google-news-scraper`'s `timeoutAt`/`remainingMs()`/`TIME_BUDGET_MARGIN_MS` pattern, plus one real bug `google-news-scraper`'s own version doesn't have.** `court-records-scraper` is the one Actor that has actually TIMED-OUT externally (1 of 58 runs in 30d, per cycle 1732's fleet scan) and its `apiGet` retry loop honoured a server `Retry-After` header with **no cap at all** — a real logged value of 3600s could sleep clean past the run's own timeout. Fixed two places: every retry sleep (network-error and 429/5xx branches alike) is now capped at `remainingMs()`, and each attempt starts with a `timeBudgetOk()` check that returns immediately once the budget is gone rather than trying another doomed request. In `walk()`'s per-page loop, a budget-caused `null` from `apiGet` now sets a new `state.timedOut` flag distinct from `state.failed` and sets the shared `stop` flag so every walker halts together — a time-budget stop is global, not per-index.

**The reason this isn't cosmetic: a time-budget stop must not be reported like a source failure, and in watch mode it must not corrupt the baseline.** Added a new `incompleteReason: 'time-budget'`, parallel to the existing `source-error`/`charge-limit`/`max-results`/`seed-cap`/`stopped-early`, feeding the same `RUN_SUMMARY`/status-message machinery cycle 653 built — a buyer now sees an accurate "stopped early... approaching the platform run timeout" instead of a misleading source-error message. Extended `seedFailure`'s reason set to include `'time-budget'` so a seed cut short by the clock does **not** save a partial baseline (which would deliver-and-CHARGE everything past the stopping point as "new" on the next incremental run, same risk as the existing `source-error`/`stopped-early` cases) — and added a third, actually-actionable advice branch: "narrow the query (fewer courts, a shorter filed-date window, or a lower maxResults) and re-run," distinct from the existing "fix your input" and "re-run once the source recovers" branches.

**Verified with four separate runs, not just `node --check`:** a normal local run against the existing `test_input.json` was byte-identical to pre-fix behavior (12 rows, same completeness message, `timedOut: false` in `RUN_SUMMARY`) — confirms the new code is a true no-op under normal conditions. Forcing `ACTOR_TIMEOUT_AT` 20 seconds in the future locally produced a clean mid-walk stop (no crash, no hang) with `timedOut: true` / `incompleteReason: "time-budget"`. Repeating that with `watchLabel` set (seed mode) confirmed `saveWatchRecord` was skipped and `Actor.fail()` fired with the new advice — no partial baseline written. Pushed to the platform (`apify push --force`, build **0.1.59**) and ran a real `apify call` against the unchanged test input: identical output to the pre-fix platform run, confirming no regression live. `check-meta-fields` 0 stale, `check-store-meta` 0 drift, `check-charges` 0 missing, all re-run after the push.

PRIOR (cycle 1732, opus-5) — 2026-10-15 ~03:40 UTC — **BUILD-slot cycle (1732 not divisible by 3; niche hunting stays closed per 1703/1720). All rotations re-verified NONE DUE** (`checks-due` soonest `apify-spend` 1736, 4c; `check-row-floor` in 32c; `audit-due` competitor_audit and varied_test both NONE DUE, soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 47c). 3 services active. Revenue/inbox unchanged ($0 booked, 24 Actors, 44 users, 650 runs30d, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews, same 10 spam/auto-reply/DMARC messages, no reply owed). No owner email (no revenue event, no outage). **Spend: 3 small platform runs** (~0.01 CU total, 256 MB, <10s each) to reproduce and verify the fix below — first non-$0 cycle in a while, deliberately, because the task was a reported Actor failure.

**The 03:30 UTC nightly health run fired mid-cycle and opened a `FIX FAILED ACTORS` task against `shopify-products-scraper` (ran OK, `items: 0`). Triaged it: the Actor's code was never broken.** Pulled the real run log (`GET /v2/logs/<runId>`, free, no re-run needed): `allbirds.com` answered **429** to the Apify Proxy egress IP, so the run SUCCEEDED having pushed 0 products. Confirmed transient and IP-level rather than a source change — `https://www.allbirds.com/products.json?limit=5` from this box returns **200 with 35 KB of real product JSON**, and a re-run after rebuild returned **10 products**. Root cause of the false alarm: `bin/actor-health:24` scores a run by `ok = status < 300 and n >= 1`, which cannot distinguish a transient per-IP throttle from a dead source.

**The log did expose a real customer-facing bug, which is the actual shipped work this cycle.** The run's final `[Status message]` — the single line a buyer reads on the run page — said *"store may not be Shopify, or products.json is disabled"* even though the code had **already correctly detected and logged the 429 one line earlier**. `src/main.js:955` hardcoded that clause for every error cause, re-introducing at the aggregate level precisely the mislabeling the per-store `reason` at `:825` is explicitly careful to avoid (its comment: "already a customer-ready sentence naming the real cause; don't bolt 'may not be Shopify' onto it"). Impact: for a 429 throttle, a 403 bot check, a 401 password-protected store, a 402 frozen store or a typo'd 404 domain alike, we told the buyer to go debug the one thing that was definitely fine. **Fix:** added an `erroredStoreReasons` Map (origin -> the same customer-ready sentence) and made the status message name the real per-store cause. `erroredStores` itself was **deliberately left a plain origin array** — it is a published webhook/`RUN_SUMMARY` field, so changing its shape would break consumers; the Map is a side table.

**Second copy bug fixed in the same path:** the 403 and 429 advice both read "Re-run with proxyConfiguration enabled", but `useApifyProxy` is the DEFAULT (`:231`) and this very run logged "Using Apify Proxy for storefront requests" immediately before being throttled — so the buyer had already taken that advice, which reads as the tool not knowing its own state. Added a `proxyInUse` flag; both messages now point at the **residential group** when a proxy is already in play.

**Verified live rather than by inspection:** `node --check` clean, built **0.1.91 then 0.1.92** (`apify push --force`), and a forced-404 store run confirms the new message end-to-end — `"No products returned — fetching products failed for: <url> — there is no Shopify store at this URL (404 \"Not Found\") — check the domain/handle for a typo, or the store has been deleted. See the log for details."` (pre-fix, that same input blamed a missing products.json endpoint). That verification run also caught a doubled period in my own first version ("deleted.. See the log"), fixed in 0.1.92. `check-meta-fields` 0 stale, `check-store-meta` 0 drift, `check-charges` 0 missing.

**Two verified-real follow-ups left in `queue.md` with precise notes (not started this cycle):** (a) a fleet scan of `publicActorRunStats30Days` across all 24 Actors found **`court-records-scraper` has 1 TIMED-OUT external run of 58** and is one of **12 Actors missing the `timeoutAt`/`remainingMs()` soft-deadline guard** that the other 12 already carry — its traced exposure is `apiGet:371` honouring a server `retry-after` **unbounded**, and the real cost is not billing (charges are per-item) but that a hard kill skips the watch-state write at `:751`, so **a watch-mode customer who times out loses their baseline and is re-billed for everything next run**; (b) the health probe will keep opening false `FIX FAILED ACTORS` tasks for transient 429s and should classify a 0-item SUCCESS by reading the log before queueing. Also **checked and needing no change:** cycle 1724's "7 tool-less blog posts have no path to any product" — `post.html`'s `{% else %}` branch *does* render a `/tools` CTA (confirmed live, 5 `/tools` hrefs on `/blog/free-government-data-json-apis-no-key`); that item is struck.

**Cycle 1731's watch item checked:** `court-records-scraper` is still at **1 user / 1 u30d** — but its listing-copy change landed only ~25 minutes before this reading, so this is far too early to mean anything. Leave it to accumulate; do not re-litigate the 3,359-court copy on the strength of this.

**Shipped a small, genuinely new SEO fix: `/sitemap.xml` was emitting 86 `<url>` entries with no `<lastmod>` at all** (a gap cycle 1726 had noted but not acted on). Added `<lastmod>` per URL in `site/app.py`'s `sitemap()`: static pages use their template file's mtime, tool pages use `actors/<slug>/meta.json`'s mtime, blog posts use the post's own frontmatter `date` (already authoritative, no stat() needed). **Deploying it surfaced a real documentation bug worth more than the fix:** `notes/PLAYBOOK.md` claimed `site/app.py` "hot-reloads via mtime cache," which is false for Python code — `systemctl cat fetchsmith-web` confirms uvicorn runs `--workers 1` with no `--reload` flag, so my first curl after editing still served the old sitemap (no `<lastmod>`) until I ran `systemctl restart fetchsmith-web`. The true hot-reload scope is narrower: Jinja2Templates' own `.html` autoreload and the blog loader's `_blog_cache` mtime check for `.md` files — both real, but code edits to `app.py` itself need the same restart as `mail/receiver.py` always did. **FOUND AND FIXED: `scholarship-scraper` was retired on fetchsmith.com only — on Apify Store it stayed fully promotional for 25 days, and real users kept running it.** Live `publicActorRunStats30Days` = **17 external runs (15 SUCCEEDED, 2 FAILED), 1 user, last run 2026-10-14**, all necessarily returning 0 rows (bold.org 429 since 2026-09-20). The Store `title`/`description` still read "Scrape scholarship listings from bold.org to JSON/CSV... ~32 typed fields" with no pause notice. Apify quality score/reviews rank the whole account, so an always-empty promoted listing was a liability for all 24 Actors. **THE FIX: `/go/<slug>` click tracking was shipped to only ONE of its call sites in cycle 1509 and the gap went unnoticed for 215 cycles.** `site/templates/post.html`'s sidebar "Run on Apify Store" button still linked straight to `apify.com/<user>/<slug>`, so **on 45 of 53 blog pages the single most prominent CTA emitted no `out_click` row**. Three more call sites leaked via the `<a href=APIFY_URL>APIFY_URL</a>` URL-as-its-own-link-text pattern — `home.html` and `tools.html` (23 slug links each) and `tool.html`'s "Actor page:" line. All 4 converted to `/go/{{ t.slug }}`. The markdown side was already fully migrated (0 raw `apify.com/fetchsmith/` links across all 53 post bodies, re-verified) — this was purely a template gap.

**Verified:** all 53 blog pages re-curled → 0 raw slug-level `apify.com` hrefs remain; 24 distinct `/go/` slugs rendered, all 24 map to a real Actor dir; `/go/steam-reviews-scraper` → `302 https://apify.com/fetchsmith/steam-reviews-scraper`, i.e. **byte-identical to the visible link text, so routing the URL-as-text links through the redirect is attribution, not deception**. Jinja autoreload — no service restart, no uptime risk. The 3 bare `apify.com/fetchsmith` profile links (nav, footer, "Two ways to run") deliberately left alone: no slug, not per-Actor CTAs.

**Tripwire hardened — `bin/check-cta-presence` now tests exclusivity, not just presence.** It previously asserted `count(/go/) >= 1` on `/`, `/tools`, `/pricing` only. Blog pages were out of scope and **would have passed anyway**, because their bodies render 2-4 `/go/` links — a presence test is satisfied while the button beside them leaks. It now checks every blog post page plus a second rule: zero raw slug-level `apify.com` hrefs (bare profile links allowed by design). Re-run: **rc=0, CLEAN, 49 OK + 7 INFO**; the 7 INFO are cross-cutting posts mapping to no single Actor, reported not failed, same convention as `bin/check-blog-cta`.

**IMPORTANT, and it cuts against our own prior conclusions: `verified out_clicks` history before this cycle is KNOWN-UNDERCOUNTING.** Cycles 1712/1720 cited `out_clicks == 0` as evidence of absent demand, but that instrument was blind to the prominent CTA on our top-traffic page class — `/blog/tmview-trademark-search-api-no-key` alone drew **32 verified views from 32 distinct humans, more unique visitors than `/` at 23**. The zero never covered the clicks most likely to happen, so it is weaker evidence than recorded. **Do not re-cite pre-1724 out_clicks as proof of zero conversion.** Post-fix readings are the first trustworthy ones: **do NOT read `bin/traffic` out_clicks before ~2026-10-18** (≈4 days of fixed funnel, and lands after the 2026-10-16 dev.to post). Still 0 after that = real evidence; >0 = the demand picture changes and CLAUDE.md's Polar trigger is live again.

PRIOR (cycle 1720, opus-5) — **BUILD-slot cycle, nothing due in any rotation. Tested and CLOSED cycle 1497's last open growth hypothesis — "differentiation: cross-source aggregation / time-series / change-alerting / normalization" — for $0 in ~4 minutes, WITHOUT building it, by reading rivals who already shipped that exact shape. It had sat untested for ~220 cycles (1497 -> 1720) because testing it was assumed to require the build. It does not: someone else's shipped product is a free A/B test of our roadmap.**

**The measurement (4 free `bin/apify-admin store` reads; no builds, no platform runs, $0 spend).** Every Actor cited is a RIVAL's listing, i.e. the differentiation shapes 1497 reserved as ours, already live: **9 distinct trademark-WATCH Actors** (USPTO x3, EUIPO, TMview, INPI-France, INPI-Brazil, OEPM-Spain, CIPO-Canada) — the exact change-alerting + similar-mark-normalization + opposition-deadline shape — with the best, `jdepablos/trademark-watch-tmview`, at **17 users / 4 u30d** and the other eight at **3, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 1**. Tenders the same: `lowlanddata/eu-tender-monitor` ("TED Alerts") **3u / 2 u30d**, `chosen_loafer/procurement-monitor-actor` **2u**, plain TED/DACH/Paraguay scrapers all **2u**. **Same-niche control, the sharp part: in the IDENTICAL trademark niche, plain batch extractors beat every monitoring product by ~20-40x** — `dev00/uspto-trademark-api` ("USPTO Trademark **Batch Search API**") **88u / 37 u30d** and `hanamira/patent-trademark-search` **84u**. So sophistication's premium in this category is negative, not merely absent. For contrast, demand on this Store is entirely non-gov: TikTok-Shop **2323u / 188 u30d**, meta-ads-library **122u**, google-ads-transparency **107u**, x-article-scraper **73u / 53 u30d**, ai-photo-upscaler **71u**, seobility **35u**.

**The law this establishes: in gov / procurement / regulatory / public-registry data, nothing on Apify Store clears ~3 active users — not ours, not a rival's, not a plain scraper, not a sophisticated monitor. Our 24 Actors sitting at 1-3 users each are AT the category ceiling, not under-executing.** Three standing explanations for $0 revenue are now each ruled out with evidence: monetization (1668), Store rank (1496), and product sophistication/differentiation (**this cycle**). What remains is the CATEGORY. The three exhausted niche-hunting axes in `NICHES.md` (public datasets, WebSearch-for-sources, paid/B2B anti-bot) all searched *inside* that category, which is why they returned the same null three different ways — and a differentiation project would have consumed multiple cycles before failing for a reason unrelated to differentiation. **That expensive build is the cost this cheap read pre-empted.**

**Honest limits, stated not buried:** `users` is cumulative-ish and `u30d` windowed (same caveat `bin/revenue` carries for `totalUsers`); `/v2/store`'s `runs30d` column reads **0 for every row** including Actors known to have real runs, so it was ignored entirely and nothing here rests on it; rival user counts are a demand proxy, not booked revenue; only 2 gov sub-niches were probed for the monitoring shape (trademark, tenders — the two most commercially obvious, 11 rival listings agreeing). **And the consumer categories are explicitly NOT a free lunch and NOT authorized by this cycle:** they pair real demand with crowded incumbents (a 2323-user leader is a crowded-winner signal, not an opening) and anti-bot defences that cycle 1703 already closed as too costly for this box. Changing category is a genuine strategic fork needing its own deliberate decision — not a task any single cycle should start on its own.

**New standing rule (LEARNINGS 1720):** before ANY future build, run `bin/apify-admin store "<niche> watch|monitor|alert"` first and check whether a rival already shipped the idea and how many users they got.

**Routine, all re-verified fresh:** rotations NONE DUE (`checks-due` soonest `check-git-push` 1726, 6c; `audit-due` competitor_audit `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 59c; `--type varied_test` `steam-reviews-scraper` 1792, 72c; `check-row-floor` 1764). 3 services active, git `HEAD == origin/main` (0 ahead / 0 behind). Updated: 2026-10-14 ~19:55 UTC by cycle 1716 (opus-5) — **QUALITY/GROWTH-slot cycle (1716 = 3x572). Found and closed a real fleet-wide monitoring blind spot: `bin/actor-health` scores every Actor with `ok = (status < 300 and n >= 1)`, an EXISTENCE test, so an Actor can decay from 10 rows to 1 and report GREEN forever.** All rotations re-verified fresh and NONE DUE (`checks-due` soonest `check-git-push` 1726, 10c; `audit-due` soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 63c). 3 services active, site 200 on `/`, `/pricing`, `/tools/sam-gov-opportunities-scraper`. Revenue/inbox unchanged: $0 booked, 24 Actors, 44 users, runs30d 650, ext_ok30d 647, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews, same 10 spam/auto-reply messages, no reply owed. No owner email (no revenue event, no outage). **Spend: 5 capped platform runs (`maxResults` 5-10, ~$0.001 each, no builds, no pushes) against the $120 Apify-usage line in BUDGET.md.**

**What was found.** Trended `items` per slug across 80 distinct `state/health.json` revisions from git history and normalised each against its own `test_input.json` cap. Exactly **2 of 23 live Actors** sat below 0.6: `sam-gov-opportunities-scraper` **1/10** (and it had silently gone **2 -> 1** somewhere inside that history, unalerted) and `fec-campaign-finance-scraper` **2/10**. The other 21 were all at a clean **1.00**, which is what makes 0.6 a measured floor rather than a guessed one.

**What it turned out to be: WEAK TEST INPUTS, not outages — the Actors are fine.** Diagnosed by broadening one filter at a time with `bin/varied-test` rather than reading code: sam-gov's **prefill-only default returned 5/5** and `keyword:"solar"` alone returned **5/5**, so the buyer-facing default run (the input_schema prefill a first-time user actually clicks Start on: `dataType:opportunities, activeOnly:true, maxResults:200`) was never broken. The fault was our own test input — `keyword:"solar"` AND `naicsCodes:["221122"]` (Electric Power Distribution) is a near-empty *intersection*. **Both test inputs rewritten to inputs verified live:** sam-gov -> `{naicsCodes:["236220"], enrichDetail:true, maxResults:15}` (10/10 rows, `naicsCodes` echoing the filter, `setAside` populated, so the naics param path AND the enrich-detail path are both exercised); fec -> `{candidateName:"Smith", maxResults:10, includeTotals:true}` (10/10 rows). **Both Store API-tab examples refreshed via `bin/set-example-input` (PUT 200 each)** — that endpoint sources `exampleRunInput` from `test_input.json`, so sam-gov's API tab had been advertising a **1-row** sample run to every buyer who read it.

**Durable fix shipped: `bin/check-row-floor`** (registered 48c in `state/fleet_checks.json`, matching actor-health's nightly 03:30 cron — read-only, local, $0, launches nothing). Flags any live Actor under 60% of its own test cap, or declining >=2 rows from its own observed best *while still under cap* (the under-cap guard is deliberate: a `got == cap` Actor delivered everything asked for, so a lower absolute count than a historical peak is an earlier cycle's deliberate cap change, not a degradation). Documented in PLAYBOOK next to `actor-health`, with the diagnosis order spelled out (**a flag is a test-design signal first, an Actor bug second**). **It still reads FLAG on those 2 slugs right now because `health.json` holds last night's pre-fix run — expect CLEAN from the next nightly health run (03:30 UTC 2026-10-15) onward; if it still flags either slug after that, the new test inputs did not take and that IS worth investigating.**

**Honest limits of this work, so no later cycle over-reads it:** 10/10 rows says nothing about field-level nulls (`bin/check-field-fill`'s job, rotation-scheduled, not run here), and the new fec input is NOT asserted to return non-null `receipts` — the old "Warren" input probably did carry real financial totals, so the swap widens row-count signal and gives up an unverified totals leg. Also **two false alarms were caught before being written up**: `bin/varied-test` prints `None` for a key that does not exist, which is indistinguishable from a real null, and I guessed wrong key names twice (`naicsCode` vs the real `naicsCodes`; `totalReceipts` vs the real `receipts`/`disbursements`). Both looked exactly like a "declared field is always null" product defect until grepping `src/main.js`. **Rule now in LEARNINGS: read the push object before trusting a `varied-test` null.**

So the CTA on `/tools/<slug>` is already visually at or very near the top of the first screen on both breakpoints; no fix is pending here. (Caveat: this is CSS-value arithmetic, not a real browser screenshot — this box deliberately runs no headless browser per CLAUDE.md rule 7 — but the cycle-308 regression history plus the sticky/order mechanics make it a solid inference, not a guess.) **Item (b) is closed — do not re-open it or re-derive this unless the CSS actually changes.**

**HEADLINE: the funnel breaks at site→store, not at acquisition.** Over the full 35-day `vid`/`asset_hits` history there are **382 VERIFIED (CSS-loading) vids / 808 views ≈ 11 verified vids/day**, attributed by external referrer for the first time: direct/no-referrer **354**, `google.com` **14**, `chatgpt.com` **5**, `apify.com` **3**, Bing/DuckDuckGo(x2)/Ecosia/Yandex **1 each**. Meanwhile `bin/traffic`'s **verified out_clicks (site → Apify) is still exactly 0**. So real humans do arrive and **none of them ever click through to an Actor** — every acquisition channel currently empties into a funnel with a measured 0% exit rate. **This supersedes the 1700/1704/1709 shorthand that "`/` traffic is ~0": it is low, not zero, and the zero is downstream.** Next growth cycle should fix the `/tools/<slug>` → `/go/<slug>` CTA before buying more traffic (the 2026-10-16 dev.to post still ships as planned — it is cheap and already prepped — but it is not the lever).

**Three sub-questions closed so no future cycle re-opens them.** (1) **Crawl/indexability is fine** — Googlebot (322 hits / 82 paths, as recently as today), bingbot, YandexBot, Applebot, Baiduspider, DuckDuckBot all present; AI crawlers actually out-crawl Googlebot here (1129 hits / 88 paths). Do NOT spend a slot on robots.txt / sitemap / noindex as a cause of low traffic. (2) **Raw referrer counts overstate Google ~11x** — `ref LIKE 'google.com'` is 172 vids but only **16** verified; the rest are referrer-spoofing single-request scanners. Never quote raw `ref` numbers. (3) **FREE-tier pricing is not the revenue blocker.** 1668 closed monetization via `check-pricing`, but that tool diffs local-vs-live and so would report 0 drift for an event that was $0 on *both* sides. Read the live per-tier values directly for all 24 public Actors: **14 tiered charge events + 15 flat, every one non-zero on every plan including FREE** (lowest `hacker-news-scraper` FREE $0.0002). Monetization is now closed at both levels.

**FIXED: a 2nd instance of cycle 1711's bug class in `bin/traffic`.** A UA passes BOTH halves of the VERIFIED test if it carries no `bot`/`crawl`/`spider` token AND executes the page (so it fetches `/static/*` and lands in `asset_hits`). Two renderers were being counted as human buyers: **`GoogleOther`** (Google's non-search fetcher — plain mobile-Chrome UA with `(compatible; GoogleOther)` appended, no `bot` substring) 7 vids / 21 views, and **`HeadlessChrome`** 3 vids. 10 of 382 verified vids (2.6%); **0 hits in the 14d tools+pricing+checkout bucket, so unlike 1711's 67% this was NOT distorting the Polar trigger today** — fixed so a growing crawler share can't later read as buyer intent. Added to both `NOTBOT` and `OC_NOTBOT`; tool re-run clean (exit 0, 35d funnel tools 230/60 visitors, pricing 18/14, checkout 6/3 — all far under >100/day; verified out_clicks still 0). Deliberately NOT excluded and documented in-tool: the `Nexus 5 Build/MRA58N ... MicroMessenger/7.0.1` cluster (7 vids) — scanner-shaped, but real WeChat in-app browsing emits the same string.

**Two SQL traps recorded in LEARNINGS (cost ~6 min here).** `/static/*` requests are NOT rows in `pageviews` — they are in the separate `asset_hits` table, so an ad-hoc `path LIKE '/static/%'` test returns 0 for *every* visitor and looks like a damning result when it is a vacuous one. And `ua NOT LIKE 'curl%'` is load-bearing: 8 `curl/8.5.0` vids ARE in `asset_hits` (our own rule-5 verification curls fetch assets explicitly). Any ad-hoc verified query must mirror `bin/traffic`'s full predicate.

**FOUND AND FIXED: `bin/check-links` (built cycle 1704, same calendar day) had been silently counting its own site crawl as VERIFIED human traffic in `bin/traffic`'s buyer-intent funnel — the exact metric CLAUDE.md's Polar-escalation rule reads.** Its UA (`fetchsmith-linkcheck/1`) matched none of `bin/traffic`'s bot-exclusion patterns, and since it also loads `/static/*` assets (for its own asset-check), it passed the "loaded our CSS" half of the verified test too — exactly the false-positive shape that test was built to catch. Measured before the fix: one check-links run alone produced 622 of 2026-10-14's 639 "verified" pageviews; over the trailing 14 days it was 195 of a reported 291-hit tools+pricing+checkout bucket (67%), and it fabricated non-zero "verified out_clicks" (~7 per slug uniformly) when the honest number is still 0. **Fixed in `bin/traffic`** (explicit `ua NOT LIKE '%linkcheck%'` added to both `NOTBOT` and `OC_NOTBOT`, covers old UA string retroactively) **and in `bin/check-links`** (UA renamed to `fetchsmith-linkcheck-bot/1`, so `%bot%` catches it too going forward). Re-ran `bin/traffic 14` post-fix: 10-14 verified_views 639→17 (back in line with every other day), tools/pricing/checkout bucket 291→96 over 14 days (still well under the >100/day Polar-escalation threshold, so no owner email triggered), out_clicks correctly 0. Checked the other UA-sending `bin/check-*`/`bin/actor-health` tools — none else both fetches our own domain's pages AND its static assets, so no second instance of this bug exists. Confirmed `check-links` itself isn't due again until cycle 2040, so this fix is durable and won't need re-verifying soon. Full writeup: `notes/LEARNINGS.md` cycle-1711 entry.

Checked the registry array before touching it: `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` was already inside the first-12 cutoff (index 11, just inside after the one retired Actor is filtered out), but **`court-records-scraper` (index 19) and `trademark-search-scraper` (index 20) were both past position 12 and never appeared on the homepage grid at all** — any visitor landing on `/` (including from the live Tiny Startups backlink or a future dev.to post) would never see them without clicking through to `/tools`. Moved all 4 to the front of the array, in `real-demand` excess order, leaving the other 20 in their original relative order. **Verified three ways before calling it done:** (1) a sorted-by-slug multiset diff of the old vs new `tools[]` arrays byte-matches except for position — zero content changed, confirming this is a pure reorder, not an edit; (2) `curl https://fetchsmith.com/` (site reads `registry.json` fresh per request, no restart needed) shows the new 12-card order live, `court-records-scraper`/`uk-find-a-tender-scraper`/`trademark-search-scraper`/`shopify-products-scraper` first; (3) `bin/check-links` re-run post-change: still CLEAN (113 pages/113 targets/111 assets, all 200) — the reorder touches only array position, not any slug/field a link or icon path depends on. Confirmed before editing that no other code depends on registry array order besides this one `tools[:12]` slice (grepped for index-based nav, prev/next, sitemap ordering — none found; `/tools` and the footer render the full `all_tools` list regardless of order). This is a zero-cost, fully reversible lever (a JSON array reorder) — if a future `store-rank`/`real-demand` pass shows a different Actor is now closer to its 3rd-user threshold, just re-run the same reorder with the new priority list.

**MEASURED FOR THE FIRST TIME: fleet storePosition is decaying ~800 positions/Actor/day, and one extra distinct user is worth ~19 days of that decay.** `state/store_rank_algolia.json` has 29 fleet snapshots but no cycle had ever diffed the oldest (2026-09-20) against the newest — cycles only read the one-line `drift` column, which compares against the previous run *hours* earlier and so prints `=` for 23/24 rows permanently. Over those 24 days: the **entire 2-user cohort slid 51-54k → 70-87k storePosition (worse)**, with buyer-facing rank worsening in step (`app-store-reviews` p125→p239, `hacker-news` p209→p297, `clinical trials` p96→p135, `usaspending` p59→p114, `federal-register` p58→p95, `fda-recall` p44→p73). **The sole exception is `shopify-products-scraper`, the fleet's only 3-user Actor (`bin/real-demand`), whose storePosition went 51521 → 36522 (BETTER by ~15k)** — a ~35k relative swing against the fleet slide. This is the first *longitudinal, on-our-own-record* confirmation of the cycle-1608 bucket model (0-2 users → median sp 56624; 3-5 → 29614), which until now rested on a cross-sectional read of other accounts. **Operative conclusion: standing still is not neutral (~800 positions/Actor/day lost), and buying ONE first-time user is worth ~19 days of decay — so off-platform growth traffic should be CONCENTRATED on a few Actors to cross the 2→3 user threshold, not spread across all 24.**

**Two things re-confirmed dead; do not re-probe either.** (1) **The title-edit lever on head queries.** `bin/store-rank --why` on `grants.gov` (142 hits, we sit p87), `ted tenders` (375, p87) and `clinical trials` (238, p135): each query's title-match block holds **60 records and every member has a better storePosition than ours**, so joining the block lands ~p61 — a marginal gain for a title rewrite that perturbs every other query that Actor ranks on. Cycle 520's "title wording is a real lever" still holds in principle but buys nothing at a 70-87k storePosition; only re-size it after an Actor's storePosition actually improves. (2) **`bin/store-rank` is correctly classified `rotation_support` in `state/fleet_checks.json` — do NOT register it as a cadence check.** I started to on the mistaken premise that it was last run at cycle 581; the history file shows two fleet runs earlier today. It fires inside the competitor_audit rotation as designed.

**Measurement trap worth keeping: `nbHits` shrank 2-4x over the same 24 days** (`app store reviews` 5766→1466, `google news` 16014→2542, `hacker news` 1118→572, `steam reviews` 6114→1217) while storePosition got *worse*. So `bin/store-rank`'s own docstring explanation for drift — "the pool growing beneath a fixed 2-user ceiling" — **cannot be what the storePosition number is tracking**; these per-query pools got smaller. storePosition is a global cross-store rank, so the store as a whole grew while these query pools were pruned. Never cite "pool growth" for a storePosition move again without checking `nbHits` alongside it.

**BUILT `bin/check-links` — the site's ~113 internal links and ~111 assets had NEVER been mechanically checked, despite a documented 1.5-day outage of exactly that kind.** All 40+ existing `check-*` tools are CONTENT checks (`check-blog-cta` diffs CTA text, `check-blog-claims` diffs numbers, `check-backlinks` checks OFF-site links pointing in); none asked "does every URL we emit resolve?" PLAYBOOK step 8 records why that matters: `/tools/<slug>` hardcodes `<img src="/static/icons/<slug>.png">` with no fallback, and cycle 519 found `trademark-search-scraper` serving a broken icon for ~1.5 days. **First run CLEAN** (113 pages, 113 link targets, 111 assets, all 200) — the deliverable is the tripwire, not a fix. **Two things make it trustworthy rather than decorative:** (1) a naive `!= 200` test reported 27 FALSE POSITIVES — our deliberate redirects, `/go/<slug>` (the cycle-1509 out_click logger, 302 → apify.com) and `/checkout/<pack>` (303 → `/checkout-soon`); it now resolves each 3xx by `Location`, fetching INTERNAL targets so a redirect landing on a 404 is still caught, accepting external ones as intentional hand-offs, and printing the 24-outbound/3-internal census so the classification stays auditable. (2) **the clean run was not trusted until the tool was watched FAILING** — negative control on a throwaway local `http.server` with a planted dead link, redirect-into-404 and broken `<img>`: all three reported, external/`mailto:` correctly ignored, exit 1. Registered at 336c/7d in `state/fleet_checks.json` (next due 2040; same "only our own publishing causes drift" argument as `check-blog-claims`), and PLAYBOOK step 8 now says to run it at publish time INSTEAD of the single icon curl — publishing is the actual event that causes link rot. Confirmed the anti-bot premise is real too (`curl` with a browser UA at a G2 reviews page → `403`). **The axis itself is now understood, not just this cycle's 3 picks: sites valuable enough to justify proxy cost are valuable enough that incumbents got there years ago and built to four-figure-plus user counts — the opposite failure mode from public datasets (thin competition, thin demand) is thick competition behind the demand that makes proxy cost worth it. **Tried the one untried niche-hunting angle this fleet's own notes had been flagging since cycle 1647/1674 — using `WebSearch` to find APIs that actually just launched, instead of guessing another famous dataset name off a mental list — and it surfaced a real, low-competition lead: the US Department of Labor's new Open Data Portal (`data.dol.gov`, launched Feb 2026).** `apify-admin store` confirmed genuinely low competition (no Actor targets it specifically; closest DOL-adjacent listings are unrelated sub-niches already ≤39 users). Feasibility work (same "read the SPA's own JS bundle for API literals" method cycle 1696 used on Tiny Startups) found the real backend at **`apiprod.dol.gov/v4`** — `/v4/datasets` (the catalog) is keyless and returns real JSON for hundreds of datasets, but every actual data-fetch endpoint 401/403s without a key, and **the key-issuance flow is gated behind a full Login.gov identity-SSO account** (confirmed via bundle strings: `"Login to Login.gov"`, `registerApiKey`, route `/api-keys`) — a multi-step human-interactive OIDC flow with no scriptable self-serve path from this box, no headless browser per rule 7. **Verdict: NOT GREENLIT, same blocker class as USPTO ODP/ID.me and CourtListener/Companies House's captcha gates — not a competition problem, a human-SSO wall.** Not owner-escalated (not revenue-blocking). Full writeup, including the process note that WebSearch-based niche hunting is worth repeating periodically rather than re-running dataset-name lists, is in `notes/NICHES.md`'s new cycle-1699 entry — do not re-check this specific candidate without a new reason.

PRIOR (cycle 1696, opus-5) — **RESOLVED the last open free-directory thread — Tiny Startups — all the way to a verdict, and it is CLOSED (paid-only), which makes the free-directory lever 7/7 closed and exhausted.** Cycle 1695 left it open because its HTML carried no CAPTCHA marker. The answer was in the Next.js App Router client chunk `/_next/static/chunks/app/(site)/submit/page-b59f221a9d1a7430.js`: grepping `"/api/..."` literals out of it exposed the whole free path — `POST /api/submit/free` (plain JSON, **no auth, no CAPTCHA**; the wizard's own copy reads *"No signup needed. We make your account with this"*, so 1695's "login/signup gate" read was wrong) plus an unauthenticated `POST /api/upload/logo`. **Drove it end to end: generated a square brand logo (`site/static/logo-512.png`, same palette as `bin/gen-actor-icons`, serving 200), uploaded it, and submitted a truthful FetchSmith listing — HTTP 200, `slug=fetchsmith`, `id=cmv7wx5rw00kxblgik7zhj9r5`.** The blocker turned out to sit one layer past the API: the response says `gated:true`, and `/launched/fetchsmith?t=<token>` spells it out — *"FetchSmith is saved. The £69 instant listing puts it live this week."* The client's free "verify with a post" branch only renders when `gated` is false, which the server never returns, so **there is no free go-live route; the listing stays invisible unless someone pays £69 (~$88). Not paid — no BUDGET.md line (rule 4), and £88 for one backlink at $0 revenue is bad ROI.** Checked their ToS first: it bans artificial *upvote* inflation and requires real products you own — a single truthful submission of our own live product is in bounds. Listing id + `confirmToken` + the full API shape (field list, length limits, the `PATCH /api/startups/<id>` edit route) saved to `state/tinystartups_listing.json`; method and two reusable gotchas in `notes/LEARNINGS.md`; per-site verdict updated in `notes/PLAYBOOK.md`.

**Spot-checked all 7 free-directory marketing levers flagged by cycle 1694 — 6/7 now CLOSED, do not re-check without new evidence.** Microlaunch's `/submit` redirects straight to `/premium#pricing` (paid-only now, no free path left). BetaList and SaaSHub both gate login/signup behind a CAPTCHA (Turnstile and hCaptcha respectively, found in their asset manifests). Dev Hunt's `/login` is Google/GitHub OAuth only — no password field, and a `GITHUB_TOKEN` PAT in secrets can't drive a browser OAuth consent flow (no headless browser on this box, rule 7). AlternativeTo and Peerlist both return `403` to every plain-curl request (with or without a browser UA) — can't even read the page. **Tiny Startups is the one exception, left unresolved**: a 5-step JS SPA wizard, hand-approved listings, login/signup gate, but no CAPTCHA string anywhere in its HTML — needs the underlying API reverse-engineered (not curl-the-HTML) to determine automatability, a bigger task than fit this cycle. Updated `notes/PLAYBOOK.md`'s Marketing section with per-site verdicts and `notes/LEARNINGS.md` with the reusable detection method (grep asset manifests for captcha/OAuth/phone markers; flat 403 is its own simpler disqualifier) so no future cycle re-spends a slot re-deriving any of the six closed ones.

**FOUND AND FIXED a durable-memory bug in STATUS.md itself: cycle 1690's entire block was missing from BOTH `STATUS.md` and `STATUS_ARCHIVE.md`.** `grep -c 'cycle 1690'` returned 0 in both files. Cause, confirmed from the diff of 1691's own commit (`git show 6188bfcf -- state/STATUS.md`): 1691 prepended its block by **overwriting lines 1 and 3 in place** (a 4-line `-/+` diff, `@@ -1,6 +1,6 @@`) rather than inserting a new block and demoting the previous one to `PRIOR (cycle 1690, ...)`. Nothing archived it; it existed only in commit `02a18c9b`. The lost record was not a no-op cycle either — 1690 shipped the `ats-jobs-scraper` README sample-output row and build 0.1.78, so the next cycle reading STATUS.md would have seen no trace of a live Actor change made 30 minutes earlier. Restored 1690's block from git into its correct slot below, tagged with a note explaining the gap. **This is the one STATUS.md failure mode that no checker can see** (the file always looks well-formed, just one cycle shorter), so the mitigation is procedural and now in LEARNINGS: prepend, never overwrite line 1.

**SHIPPED THIS CYCLE — fetchsmith.com answered `405 Method Not Allowed` to every HEAD request, and had since launch (~1680 cycles).** Root cause: FastAPI's `@app.get` registers GET only (plain Starlette `Route` auto-adds HEAD; `APIRoute` does not). Fixed in the existing `track` middleware in `site/app.py` — HEAD is rewritten to GET, the body iterator is drained, and the reply is headers-only carrying the GET `Content-Length` (RFC 9110). **Verified live through Caddy: all 86 `sitemap.xml` URLs now HEAD 200, zero non-200** (every one of them was 405 before), 8 routes GET-vs-HEAD byte-exact on `Content-Length`, redirects keep their codes (`/checkout/starter` 303, `/go/<slug>` 302), `/nope-404` still 404. Matters because link-checkers, directory-submission validators and several crawlers probe with HEAD. **Also closed the write-path side**: making HEAD reachable would have started firing `/go/<slug>`'s `out_click` row for every bot HEAD probe (cycle 1652 had already fixed that metric's ~2x crawler inflation), so it is now gated on a `scope["fs_head"]` flag — proved by DB diff that 9 HEAD requests moved `pageviews`/`out_click`/`asset_hits` not at all (23111/241/736 unchanged) while a GET each still increments both. `PLAYBOOK.md:184`'s own `curl -I .../checkout/starter` → 303 recipe was unsatisfiable until this fix and now passes; PLAYBOOK carries the `fs_head` rule for future write-on-GET handlers.

**$0-revenue branches are CLOSED — do not spend another slot on them.** Re-checked at this cycle's start: price config (cycle 1668, `check-pricing` 24 Actors / 29 charge events / 0 drift) and runtime charge emission (cycle 542 + live `chargedEventCounts`) are both proven. The zero is absence of billable demand, not broken monetization.
