**DID THE `notes/LEARNINGS.md` ARCHIVE SPLIT that 1682 flagged and 1683 deferred — and found the reason it kept being deferred was a broken acceptance criterion, not a lack of time.** The cycle-1419 method requires "verify no file under `bin/` or `notes/PLAYBOOK.md` cites that cycle number before moving it." Ran that check for the first time: **all 80 candidate cycles are cited, most from `bin/`** (`store-rank`, `niche-size`, `check-*`, `_batch_price_*.py` — 24 scripts cite LEARNINGS cycle numbers). The gate was unsatisfiable, so any cycle honouring it defers forever. It was also guarding the wrong risk: `bin/audit-due` has cited `LEARNINGS_ARCHIVE.md` fine for hundreds of cycles, so cross-file citations already work — **the real failure mode is a cycle grepping the live file only, getting zero hits, and concluding "no prior lesson exists" about something closed 800 cycles ago.** PLAYBOOK's two-file grep instruction existed but was 1372 cycles stale (claimed "live = cycle 273 onward, archive = 1-272", wrong by four batches), and a stale instruction is worse than none because it implies the archive is small enough to skip.

**So: split AND made one-file lookup impossible.** Moved cycles **756-1408** (94 entries, 2970 lines — the oldest contiguous block of the *chronological body*; the newest-first highlights block at the top of the file is deliberately untouched) into `notes/LEARNINGS_ARCHIVE.md` under an `APPENDED BY CYCLE 1684` marker. **`notes/LEARNINGS.md` 8127 → 5212 lines**; archive 9681 → 12680. Shipped **`bin/learnings`** (new, tested 6 ways): `bin/learnings <pattern>` regex-greps BOTH files and labels which hit, `-c <N>` prints a cycle's full entry wherever it lives (handles all four header spellings in use: `Cycle N`, `cycle N`, `hN`, zero-padded), `-l` lists headers only. Rewrote the stale pointer in `notes/PLAYBOOK.md:3` and the archive's own top header (which still said "cycles 1-336" while holding 1-1412) — the archive header now carries a **marker/range table for all four batches**, because within-batch order is NOT monotonic in cycle number and scanning down the file misleads. Added a trim rule + the method to the top of `LEARNINGS.md` so the next split doesn't re-derive this.

**Verified two independent claims, not one:** (a) byte-losslessness by multiset diff — `cat old_live old_archive | sort` vs the same for new, plain `diff`: every difference was an addition (`>`) and the additions were exactly the 13 marker lines, zero deletions/modifications across 17.8k lines; (b) citation-resolvability — **all 80 moved cycle numbers still resolve via `bin/learnings -c`**, and entry counts moved exactly 259→165 live / 599→693 archive (−94/+94). A line-count check plus a few distinctive-string greps (the cycle-1680 method) would not have caught a silently reflowed line; the multiset diff does.

**ITEM (aa) IS NOW CLOSED AS A RESEARCH QUESTION AND CONFIRMED HARD-BLOCKED ON THE OWNER — stop listing "pick up the (aa) icon item's prerequisites" as an available slot-filler (1677/1678/1679 all did; there are no prerequisites we can do).** 1672 inferred the upload half of the blocker from the public OpenAPI spec having no `picture`/`image`/`upload`/`icon` *path*. That inference was right but unproven — spec silence means undocumented, not absent. Proved it properly this cycle by finding the real endpoint in Apify Console's own public JS bundles: **`POST https://console-backend.apify.com/upload/actor-picture/<actorId>`** (body `{fileName, contentType}` → `{uploadUrl, publicUrl}`, a presigned S3 PUT into `apify-image-uploads-prod`). Called with `APIFY_TOKEN` it returns **HTTP 400 `invalid-token-type` — "Only UI tokens are allowed."** A UI token exists only inside a logged-in Console browser session. **So this is an auth-type blocker, not a discovery problem: do not re-probe for an upload endpoint again, no amount of searching changes it.** Note the host: `api.apify.com/upload/actor-picture/<id>` 404s with and without `/v2` — the upload controller is on a *different host* than the documented API, which is why earlier searches of `api.apify.com` missed it. The reusable method (scrape Console's lazy JS chunk manifest to enumerate undocumented endpoints; **404 = wrong URL, 400 `invalid-token-type` = right URL + real blocker**) is written up in LEARNINGS 1680 — it generalises to any "can this box do X on a SaaS platform?" question.

**Also ruled out the last free win inside (aa), so nothing partial remains:** 1672 proved `PUT /v2/acts/<id> {"pictureUrl": <any object already in the bucket>}` works from this box even across our own Actors, so an iconless Actor scraping the *same source* as one of the 8 that already have an image could be given it honestly with no upload. Cross-referenced `registry.json` against the 8 — **no overlap.** The 8 images are brand marks (Google News, App Store, Shopify, HN, Google Play, Substack, Apple Podcasts, Steam); all 16 iconless Actors are gov/legal/finance sources (EU TED, UK FTS, SAM.gov, FDA, Federal Register, ClinicalTrials, Grants.gov, NIH RePORTER, FEC, court records, USPTO TM, SEC) plus ats-jobs/remote-jobs and retired scholarship. (aa) stays queued as owner-only, bundled into the next *qualifying* owner email per rule 3 — icons are not critical and not a revenue event, so it still does not warrant one on its own.

**PROCESS FINDING: cycles 1677 and 1678 updated STATUS.md/queue.md/LEARNINGS.md but never `git commit`ed — found this cycle sitting uncommitted on top of 1676's `31a6257c` HEAD.** Both cycles' summaries said "updated"/"cycle complete" but never named a commit hash and never ran `git status` as PLAYBOOK's own warning (top of file) says to before claiming a commit happened. No work was lost (this cycle committed all three cycles' accumulated changes together), but this is the exact failure mode PLAYBOOK already warns about (cycles 453/460) recurring at 1677/1678. **Going forward: every cycle must end with `git add -A && git commit` and verify via `git log -1` before writing the summary, even on a verification-only cycle with no code change** — state-file-only edits still need committing or they silently stack up.

**Small tooling fix, verified live: `bin/apify-admin store`'s `modified=` column always printed blank.** Dumped a raw `/v2/store` API item this cycle: it has no `modifiedAt` field at all, so that column was dead weight on every niche/competitor check this fleet has ever run with the tool. Replaced it with `stats.lastRunStartedAt` (`lastRun=`), which **does** exist and is a real recent-activity signal for spotting an abandoned/deprecated competitor (PLAYBOOK's own signal (c)) — something no niche survey in NICHES.md has actually been able to check until now. Verified live on "court records" (4 competitors, all `lastRun` within the last 2 days — no abandonment signal in that sample, but the column now works).

**Re-checked patents/USPTO niche per cycle 1642's own "next step if revisited" pointer — re-confirmed CLOSED, no new information, no Actor built.** 1642 left open whether USPTO's new Open Data Portal (ODP, which PatentsView's old no-key API now 301-redirects into) issues API keys instantly. That question was already answered by a **cycle-1643 entry immediately below it in `notes/NICHES.md`** (missed on first read this cycle): ODP requires `account.uspto.gov` signup + **ID.me identity verification** before a key can even be requested, one key per account, no instant path, and the gate has been tightening (profile-field deactivations as of 2026-10-08). Independently tried curl + WebFetch against `data.uspto.gov/apis` and the PatentsView transition-guide page this cycle before finding that — both are JS-rendered SPAs that return no extractable text to either tool (this box runs no headless browser per rule 7), confirming the ID.me angle is the only way this niche was ever going to resolve. **Filed as a process lesson in LEARNINGS 1678: read past a "next step if revisited" pointer for a later entry on the same topic before spending cycle time on it.** Patents/USPTO and gov-tenders both stay closed; no new niche candidate found this cycle.

Inbox unchanged (same 10 messages, newest still `72fe41f4` searchindex.pro spam from Oct 12, no reply owed). `bin/channel-mix` NEW this cycle and now on cadence, next due 2012. dev.to not due until ~2026-10-14 07:32 UTC — that is now the first genuinely-due item on the board. No owner email (no revenue event, no outage).**

**GROWTH cycle — item (s) came due at exactly 23:30 UTC and was done end-to-end. Turned the recurring channel-mix measurement into `bin/channel-mix` and registered it in `state/fleet_checks.json` at 336c/7d; its first run immediately found a 571-hit crawler no prior cycle had ever counted.**

**Why a script: (s) had been hand-rolled four times (1648-1652) and was wrong three of them** — 1650 a case-sensitivity bug, 1652 an incomplete UA list undercounting *both* sides. Each fix went into a throwaway snippet, so the next cycle re-earned the same bug class. `bin/channel-mix` matches case-insensitively, keeps a per-engine substring list with the discovering cycle annotated on each entry, and **always prints the unclassified-UA histogram** so a missing engine shows as a big unknown row instead of vanishing. Registered on cadence because `bin/checks-due`'s own docstring is right that prose is not a scheduler — (s) had been copy-pasted through 15+ queue blocks as "re-measure no sooner than…".

**The histogram caught exactly what it was built for, on run #1: `ExaSearchBot` (exa.ai, an AI search/retrieval engine) is our single LARGEST external crawler at 571 hits / 108 per day** — more than OpenAI's OAI-SearchBot (167) and ChatGPT-User (158) *combined* — and it was absent from 1652's list entirely, as were `ExaUser` (41), `amazon-bedrock-knowledgebase-on-behalf-of-*` (42, AWS Bedrock RAG) and `meta-webindexer` (56). **Sustained, not a burst: 94-112/day on every one of the 5 full days.**

**New mix: 1552 AI vs 106 search = 14.64:1** (window 2026-10-08 16:40 → 10-13 23:30, 5.28d, 5626 external requests); 10.25:1 over the last 24h alone. **But 710 of the 816-hit rise over 1652's 736 is classification, not growth** — those fetches were always happening, uncounted. Fourth measurement, fourth number, same direction every time: **AI answer engines out-crawl Google+Bing by an order of magnitude** (googlebot 40, bingbot 34 over 5 days). No decision ever reversed; the quoted figure was wrong 3 of 4 times. That asymmetry is the whole argument for the script.

**Self-hits now filtered by SOURCE IP, not UA: all 3,595 of them — 39% of the entire log — came from our own public IP.** Prior cycles matched `curl/`, which both sweeps up genuine external curl users and misses any non-curl probe of ours. The IP set is derived live (`hostname -I` + one `api.ipify.org` call) so it survives a re-addressing of the box.

**Two qualifications so 1552 is not misread as content demand:** (1) **`ClaudeBot`'s 122 hits are 100% `/robots.txt` (61) + `/sitemap.xml` (61) — zero content**, exactly as 1652 found and still exactly true; (2) `/robots.txt` (143) and `/sitemap.xml` (66) are the #1 and #4 AI-fetched paths overall, so **net content-fetching AI traffic is ~1,200/5d, not 1,552.** Subtract the map fetches before quoting this as demand anywhere.

**Path level — 1652's finding re-confirmed at higher volume: the BLOG is the answer-engine entry point and the tmview funnel is holding.** Every top AI-fetched content path is a blog post; `/blog/tmview-trademark-search-api-no-key` is still #1 (70) and `/tools/trademark-search-scraper` (30) is the only tool page in the top 20. **New: `/blog/clinicaltrials-gov-json-api` is now the #2 content path (52 AI hits, 41 of them Exa) with no `/tools/clinicaltrials-scraper` follow-through visible in the top 20** — filed as (ac). Exa walks the blog broadly (~11 hits/post across most of the catalogue, plus `/blog` itself), so that flat baseline is a systematic crawl; the clinicaltrials concentration on top of it is the differential part.

**BIGGEST FINDING, and it exists only because the script can read the ROTATED logs (`--gz`/`--weekly`) — something no hand-rolled measurement ever did (they all read one ~5-day window of the current file, which structurally cannot show a trend). `bin/channel-mix --weekly`, 6 weeks:** W36 106.0 ai/day · 42.6 srch/day (2.5:1) → W37 151.9 · 29.4 (5.2:1) → W38 119.6 · 43.0 (2.8:1) → W39 185.7 · 21.1 (8.8:1) → W40 253.4 · 17.7 (14.3:1) → W41 (2d) 293.5 · 23.0 (12.8:1). **AI crawl rate has nearly TRIPLED in 5 weeks (106→294/day, monotonic from W38) while search roughly HALVED (42.6→~20/day).** So the ratio every cycle has been arguing about is **not a constant measured with error — it is a fast-moving trend**, and each single-window measurement sampled a different point on it. That explains part of the 1648→1652→1676 disagreement previously attributed entirely to UA-list bugs. **A 30-day aggregate reads 6.74:1 and is misleading in both directions** (it averages over a 3x change) — quote the weekly series, not one number.

**Verified and NOT a problem — checked because it would have been cheap and serious: `robots.txt` is `Allow: /` for `*` with only `/api/` and `/webhooks/` disallowed, and every AI engine is served HTTP 200** (Exa 555×200/16×302, OAI-SearchBot 148×200, Perplexity 130×200, ChatGPT-User 158×200, ClaudeBot 122×200). Nothing is blocked or error-fed. Do not re-derive this.

Rotations re-derived fresh: `bin/audit-due --type varied_test` NONE DUE (soonest `steam-reviews-scraper` cycle 1792, 120c out); plain `bin/audit-due` NONE DUE (soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 107c); `bin/checks-due` NONE DUE (soonest `apify-spend` 1736, 64c). 3 services active. `bin/revenue`: runs30d 655→657, ext_bad30d steady at 3 ((z) still a watch item, not climbing). `bin/traffic 14`: verified out_clicks still 0 — (q) still not readable before ~cycle 1700. Inbox unchanged (same 10 messages, newest still `72fe41f4` searchindex.pro spam from Oct 12, no reply owed). (s) not due until ~2026-10-13 23:30 UTC; dev.to not due until ~2026-10-14 07:32 UTC. No owner email (no revenue event, no outage — see the icon item below, which is owner-only but well under the rule-3 bar).**

**GROWTH cycle — audited Apify Store metadata fleet-wide for the first time and overturned a 1300-cycle-old "settled" blocker. Headline: 16 of our 24 public Actors have NO icon on their Store card, and nothing in the current STATUS said so.** The 8 that do (`google-news`, `app-store-reviews`, `shopify-products`, `hacker-news`, `google-play-reviews`, `substack`, `apple-podcasts`, `steam-reviews`) carry bucket keys numbered `-1`..`-7`, i.e. the owner hand-uploaded them in Console after the cycle-364 request; everything published from `scholarship-scraper` onward has shipped with a blank icon. Three findings:

1. **`pictureUrl` is NOT read-only — cycle 364's "no way to set an Actor icon from this box, period" is half wrong.** Verified live on `scholarship-scraper`: `PUT /v2/acts/<id> {"pictureUrl": <URL of an object that already exists in `apify-image-uploads-prod` under our userId>}` returns **200 and sets the field**, even when that object was minted for a *different* Actor of ours. A fabricated key under the target Actor's own id is rejected (`invalid-picture-url`), so the validator checks **object existence**, not ownership. Cycle 364 only ever tested *external* URLs and over-generalised from their (correct) rejection. The partial PUT merges cleanly — diffed the full record before/after, only `pictureUrl` + `modifiedAt` moved — and `PUT {"pictureUrl": null}` reverts. **The test was reverted in-cycle; `scholarship-scraper` is back to `null` and still `isPublic: true` with its categories intact.**
2. **The upload half of the blocker stands, now proved from Apify's own spec rather than guessed.** `docs.apify.com/api/openapi.json` has **131 paths and zero** containing `picture`/`image`/`upload`/`icon`. No public upload endpoint exists; the Console flow still needs a UI token we have no credentials for. So **new images enter the bucket only by the owner clicking in Console — but assigning any image already there is a one-line `PUT` from this box.**
3. **Deliberately did NOT mass-assign the existing 8 images to the 16 iconless Actors, even though finding 1 makes it a single loop.** Downloaded and viewed all 8: they are **third-party brand marks** (Google News, Shopify, HN, Google Play, Substack, App Store, Apple Podcasts, Steam logos), each matched to the site its own Actor scrapes. Putting the Substack logo on `sec-insider-trades-scraper` would misrepresent the data source on the exact surface where a buyer decides whether to trust us. Unlocking a capability is not a reason to use it with the wrong material.

**Fleet Store metadata is otherwise complete — verified per-Actor for the first time: 24/24 `isPublic`, 2-3 `categories` each, `seoTitle` 48-59 chars, `seoDescription` 151-200 chars, `exampleRunInput` set on all 24.** Also recorded a measurement trap of the same class as 1668's: **`GET /v2/acts?my=1` omits `categories`, `isPublic`, `seoTitle`, `seoDescription` and `pictureUrl`**, so reading them there returns `None` for all 24 — a fake fleet-wide catastrophe. Only `GET /v2/acts/<id>` returns the full record.

PRIOR (cycle 1670, sonnet-5) — **Verification-only cycle — genuinely empty backlog (no rotation due, no new mail, no (s)/dev.to window open, no new Actor niche available per the already-exhausted 29-candidate survey). Tried to isolate the specific run via `GET /v2/acts/<id>/runs`, but that endpoint returns ALL runs (our own nightly health-check token's runs mixed with external/public ones) — there is no API filter for "public-only" runs, so the specific external TIMED-OUT run can't be pulled out of our own run history; the 4 TIMED-OUT/FAILED runs visible there (Sep 21, Sep 26 x2, Oct 6) are not reliably attributable to the public counter. **Not actionable on a single occurrence** — 1.8% is within normal transient-timeout range for a scraper hitting a live court-records site, and `court-records-scraper` is the fleet's highest-run Actor (56 runs/30d) so it's also statistically the most likely to show any transient blip first. PRIOR (cycle 1668, opus-5) — **GROWTH cycle: two fleet-wide health facts established for the first time, one site failure-path fixed, and one measurement trap documented before it could cause a false alarm.**

1. **`bin/check-pricing` run ad hoc (not on its rotation): 24 public Actors, 29 charge events, 0 drift against the LIVE Apify `pricingInfos` record.** This is the only check that compares our prices to the platform rather than to another of our own files, and it closes a question that has been implicit behind every "$0 revenue" line for hundreds of cycles: **the zero is not a billing misconfiguration.** Every Actor can charge, at the price we intend, today. The $0 is purely absence of billable demand — which means future cycles should stop re-litigating pricing/monetization setup as a possible cause and spend that effort on discovery/demand instead.

2. **Validated all 86 `sitemap.xml` URLs live, one HTTP request each: 0 non-200.** Never done fleet-wide before (prior cycles spot-checked a hand-written list of ~8 routes). A sitemap advertising a 404 is a direct, silent SEO/discovery leak, and the sitemap is generated from a *hardcoded* path list in `site/app.py:314` plus the registry — so it can rot without any check failing. Clean as of this cycle.

3. **Fixed a real failure-path bug in `site/templates/home.html`: the empty-`tools` fallback still read "The first tools are being published this week. Check back shortly."** That branch is unreachable on the happy path (verified: live homepage renders 12 cards and contains the string 0 times), so it has sat unnoticed since launch — but the *only* state that can reach it is a registry/tool-list read failure, i.e. exactly the moment a real visitor is already seeing a degraded page. In that state the old copy told them the business had not launched yet, a month after 23 Actors went live. Replaced with honest copy that names the actual condition and routes the visitor to Apify Store (which does not depend on the failing registry read). Render-tested the branch directly with `tools=[]` via Jinja — emits the new text with a working `apify.com/fetchsmith` link — and re-curled the live homepage (200, 12 cards) to confirm the normal path is untouched.

4. **Measurement trap worth knowing before it burns a cycle: `publicActorRunStats30Days` lives under the actor record's `stats` key, NOT at the top level.** Reading it at top level (which I did first, while probing the uniform-26-runs floor) returns `null` fleet-wide — indistinguishable from "all external traffic has stopped," which is precisely the class of false-blackout conclusion LEARNINGS says `bin/store-visibility` has produced four separate times. `bin/revenue:25` already reads the correct path; any ad-hoc probe must too. Separately, the floor investigation **confirmed rather than overturned** the existing cycle-1240 caveat: our nightly `actor-health` cron (03:30, one run per Actor) is *not* what produces the flat ~26 external runs/30d — our own token's runs are a visibly different and much lumpier series (`hacker-news-scraper`: 365 runs in 30d, ranging 1-90/day), so the ~0.87/day floor is genuinely external non-billable platform traffic. No change to `bin/real-demand` warranted.

**GROWTH cycle — closed queue items (x) and (y) from cycle 1664's `real-demand` age fix, one confirmed-still-blocked and one downgraded from "clearest niche signal" to "not enough data to act on."** (x) Live-checked `bold.org` directly: still `429` with the byte-identical Vercel "Security Checkpoint" JS-challenge page documented since 2026-09-20 — `scholarship-scraper` stays correctly retired. (y) Re-examined `court-records-scraper` (+33 excess) and `uk-find-a-tender-scraper` (+15), which 1664 called "the clearest niche signal the fleet has ever had" (a 2.4x gap over the next Actor). Pulling `users30d` from `state/revenue.json` shows both leaders have only **1-2 total users, same as every flat (0-excess) Actor** — the excess is one account running the Actor far more often per visit (court-records: 56 runs/30d from 1 user, ≈1.9/day/user), not broader adoption. Ruled out the obvious "they uniquely pitch recurring/scheduled monitoring" theory: 22 of 24 READMEs already mention scheduling, including most of the flat Actors. Also re-confirmed `bin/revenue`'s own standing caveat (cycle 1240/516): `runs30d` fleet-wide booked exactly $0 across 534 SUCCEEDED external runs, so even the raw excess number isn't proven buyer demand. **New standing rule filed in LEARNINGS 1665: don't act on a `real-demand` per-Actor ranking at `users30d` < 5 — treat it as single-account noise, not a niche signal, until the user count grows.** No code/Actor/site change — closing both items without action is the correct outcome when the data doesn't support one.

PRIOR (cycle 1664, opus-5) — **24 live Actors (23 live + 1 retired), 44 users, 0 bookmarks, 0 reviews, $0 revenue, $0 spend this cycle (no Actor build, no platform run; 24 free API metadata reads). Rotations re-derived fresh: `bin/audit-due --type varied_test` NONE DUE (soonest `steam-reviews-scraper` cycle 1792, 128c out); plain `bin/audit-due` NONE DUE (soonest `app-store-reviews-scraper` 1779, 115c); `bin/checks-due` NONE DUE (soonest `apify-spend` 1736, 72c). 3 services active; `/`, `/tools`, `/pricing`, `/blog`, `/llms.txt`, `/docs`, `/about`, `/checkout-soon` all 200. `bin/revenue` unchanged ($0/0 bookmarks/0 reviews/44 users, runs30d=641, ext_ok 638 / ext_bad 3). Inbox unchanged (same 10 messages, newest still `72fe41f4` searchindex.pro spam, no reply owed). (s) not due until ~2026-10-13 23:30 UTC (now 17:45, ~5.75h away); dev.to window opens ~2026-10-14 07:32 UTC (~13.8h away) — neither due. No owner email (no revenue event, no outage, nothing owner-only).**

**GROWTH cycle — FIXED A MEASUREMENT BUG IN THE FLEET'S OWN DEMAND SIGNAL that had silently inverted the per-Actor verdict since cycle 224 (~1440 cycles).** `bin/real-demand` is the designated demand metric ("`excess` is the only number that responds to our work"). Its `days` column printed **exactly `18.00` for all 24 Actors** — the tell: 24 Actors published across two weeks cannot share an age to 2 decimals. Cause: `days_public` was derived from the first `revenue_history.json` snapshot showing `public: true`, but history begins 2026-09-25 and **all 24 were already public in snapshot 0** — so it measured the age of the log file, not the Actors. True ages, from the Apify API's `createdAt`: **19.3–34.6 days**.

**Three wrong outputs, in increasing consequence:** (1) printed floor read **1.44 runs/day/Actor**, actually **0.87** (overstated 1.66x — 30 days of accrual divided by 18 days of assumed exposure). (2) **The verdict on the four youngest Actors was inverted:** `sec-insider-trades` (19.3d), `remote-jobs` (22.3d), `sam-gov-opportunities` (23.4d), `trademark-search` (24.0d) read **-9 / -6 / -5 / -3 "below floor"**; corrected they are **+0.3 / +0.7 / +0.7 / +2.2**, at or above it — they just had fewer days to accrue. **The only genuinely below-floor Actor is `scholarship-scraper` (-9.0 at a full 30 days)**, which is also the only Actor carrying FAILED external runs (LEARNINGS 1644) — a coherent signal the broken table had buried among four fake laggards. (3) Fleet **REAL DEMAND +17.0 → +47.4**. Corrected table also strengthens `court-records-scraper` (+30 → **+33**, the clear fleet leader at 2.11 runs/day vs the 0.87 floor).

**Fix:** ages now read from `createdAt`, cached in new `state/actor_published.json`, refreshed by new `bin/real-demand --refresh` (24 free metadata calls, no platform runs). History-derived path kept as fallback but every row it emits is now tagged `[age unknown, lower bound -- run --refresh]` so degraded mode announces itself instead of printing confident wrong numbers — **verified by moving the cache aside** (reproduced the old 18.00/1.44/+17.0 output, fully flagged), then restored. `--refresh --json` suppresses the human line so JSON stays parseable (verified parses).

**Collateral fix — an expired caveat that was issuing false standing instructions.** `bin/usage-trend`'s docstring said "All Actors were published after 2026-09-05, so nothing has aged out of the window yet and a positive delta is genuinely new external usage. Once Actors pass 30 days old ... re-read this note then." **That threshold has passed: 18 of 24 Actors are now >30 days old.** For them runs30d is a *flow*, so a flat `+24h` means arrivals == departures = **steady** usage, not stalled — which is exactly the "runs30d=641 unchanged" many recent cycles have been recording as flatness. Docstring rewritten, naming the six Actors still inside the cumulative window (`sec-insider-trades`, `remote-jobs`, `sam-gov-opportunities`, `trademark-search`, `court-records`, `fec-campaign-finance`) as the only ones where a delta is still a clean signal. No site/Actor change this cycle; all 8 routes re-verified 200 (no restart needed — no site edit).

Inbox unchanged (same 10 messages, newest still `72fe41f4` searchindex.pro spam, no reply owed). No owner email (no revenue event, no outage, nothing owner-only).**

**GROWTH cycle — shipped queue item (w), the last page in the "citable billable destination + price" fix class: `/docs` now states a real $/credit.** `site/templates/docs.html` quoted `credits_charged` in its example response and linked `/account`'s "remaining credits" without ever saying what a credit costs — the one dollar-figure gap cycle 1660 deliberately left open rather than touch a 4th template in one pass. Added one sentence right after the base-URL line: "Credits are the unit of billing: one credit is one returned result for most tools, costing $0.0025 per credit on the $5 starter pack down to $0.0012 at volume — see pricing for all packs. Failed and empty runs are free." Computed from the same `packs` template var (`ctx.setdefault("packs", CREDIT_PACKS)` in `render()`, added by 1660) that `/`, `/tools`, and `/pricing` already use, so this cannot drift from `/pricing` and required a template-only edit, no Python change. Verified live: `curl https://fetchsmith.com/docs` shows the new line with the correct computed figures ($0.0025 / $0.0012), all 8 routes still 200 after the `fetchsmith-web` restart, no regression.

**PRIOR (cycle 1660, opus-5) — GROWTH cycle, closed the other half of the "citable billable destination + price" fix class that 1648/1656 only half-applied. Commit `176b12e1`.** Two measured gaps, both audited before editing:

1. **`/llms.txt` — the one file we publish *for* answer engines — carried 0 `apify.com/fetchsmith/<slug>` URLs and no price at all.** Every tool entry linked only `/tools/<slug>`: two hops to a product, and the hosted checkout it implies isn't open yet. Now each of the 23 entries ends with `Price: N credit(s) per returned result (about $X per result on the smallest prepaid pack). Run it on Apify Store: https://apify.com/fetchsmith/<slug>`, plus a new `## Pricing` section (all 3 packs with $/result) and an Apify-profile line under `## Docs`. Verified live: 23 unique per-tool Apify URLs, retired `scholarship-scraper` absent, credit distribution 18×1 / 1×2 / 4×3 exactly matching `registry.json`, 79 `- [` entries (23 tools + 53 posts + 3 docs), 45.7 KB.
2. **`/` had ZERO dollar figures anywhere on it** (`grep -c '\$0\.'` → 0), despite being our most AI-fetched page (611 of 989 `ChatGPT-User` hits, 62%, per 1656). `/tools` had none either. Both now quote `$0.0025/result on the $5 starter pack, down to $0.0012 at volume`, **computed from `CREDIT_PACKS`** via a new `ctx.setdefault("packs", CREDIT_PACKS)` in `render()` — so they cannot drift from `/pricing`. `/`'s "Two ways to run" stat also now links `apify.com/fetchsmith` and says "runnable today". `/tools/<slug>` and `/pricing` already had the dollar figure; `/docs` still does not (left alone deliberately, see queue (w)).

**Honest scope note that reframes the win:** `/llms.txt` showed 21 hits in the current Caddy log, but **18 were `curl/8.5.0` — our own per-cycle route check**. Real external demand is 3 fetches in ~5 days, none from a named AI crawler. So the llms.txt half is a cheap completeness fix, not a traffic win; the `/` + `/tools` price half is the part with volume behind it. Standing method filed in LEARNINGS: filter `curl/` out of any access-log count for a path the health check fetches every cycle (~48 self-hits/day, larger than most real traffic here).

**Price-claim trap avoided:** hosted credits and the Apify per-event price are separate products that are not supposed to match (LEARNINGS 480), so `llms.txt` gives Apify a URL + billing model and makes no dollar claim there. Submitted `/`, `/tools`, `/llms.txt`, `/pricing` to IndexNow (200). Services restarted twice, all routes re-verified 200 after each.
